        if not v.startswith("CVSS:4.0/"):
            raise ValueError("cvss_estimate must be N/A or a CVSS:4.0 vector")
        return v

# ---------- System prompt ----------------------------------------------------

SYSTEM_PROMPT = Path(__file__).parent.joinpath("triage_system.txt").read_text()
FEWSHOTS = json.loads(Path(__file__).parent.joinpath("fewshots.json").read_text())

# ---------- Scope ------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass
class ScopeVerdict:
    in_scope: bool
    citation: str

def load_scope(path: str) -> dict:
    with open(path) as f:
        return yaml.safe_load(f)

def check_scope(scope: dict, asset: str, cwe: str) -> ScopeVerdict:
    import fnmatch
    for entry in scope.get("out_of_scope", []):
        if fnmatch.fnmatch(asset, entry["pattern"]):
            return ScopeVerdict(False, f"scope.yaml out_of_scope: {entry['pattern']} — {entry['reason']}")
    for entry in scope.get("excluded_classes", []):
        if entry["cwe"] != cwe:
            continue
        if entry["when"] == "always":
            return ScopeVerdict(False, f"scope.yaml excluded_classes: {cwe} always — {entry['reason']}")
        if entry["when"].startswith("on "):
            pat = entry["when"][3:]
            if fnmatch.fnmatch(asset, pat):
                return ScopeVerdict(False, f"scope.yaml excluded_classes: {cwe} on {pat} — {entry['reason']}")
    for entry in scope.get("in_scope", []):
        if fnmatch.fnmatch(asset, entry["pattern"]):
            return ScopeVerdict(True, f"scope.yaml in_scope: {entry['pattern']}")
    return ScopeVerdict(False, "asset does not match any in_scope entry")

# ---------- Embeddings and duplicates ----------------------------------------

EMBED_MODEL = SentenceTransformer("all-MiniLM-L6-v2")

def embed(text: str) -> np.ndarray:
    v = EMBED_MODEL.encode([text])[0]
    return v / np.linalg.norm(v)

def cosine(a: np.ndarray, b: np.ndarray) -> float:
    return float(np.dot(a, b))

def load_history(path: str) -> list[dict]:
    out = []
    if not os.path.exists(path):
        return out
    with open(path) as f:
        for line in f:
            row = json.loads(line)
            row["embedding"] = np.array(row["embedding"], dtype=float)
            out.append(row)
    return out

def find_duplicate(new_finding: dict, history: list[dict]) -> tuple[dict, float] | None:
    e_new = embed(new_finding["title"] + "\n\n" + new_finding["body"])
    best = None
    for h in history:
        sim = cosine(e_new, h["embedding"])
        if sim < 0.87:
            continue
        if not set(new_finding["assets"]) & set(h["assets"]):
            continue
        if not set(new_finding["cwes"]) & set(h["cwes"]):
            continue
        if best is None or sim > best[1]:
            best = (h, sim)
    return best

# ---------- LLM call and self-consistency ------------------------------------

client = Anthropic()

def build_user_message(finding: dict, scope_citation: str) -> str:
    ex = "\n\n".join(
        f"EXAMPLE INPUT:\n{s['input']}\n\nEXAMPLE OUTPUT:\n{json.dumps(s['output'])}"
        for s in FEWSHOTS
    )
    return (
        f"{ex}\n\n"
        f"SCOPE CITATION:\n{scope_citation}\n\n"
        f"REPORT:\n{finding['title']}\n\n{finding['body']}\n\n"
        f"ASSETS: {finding['assets']}\nCWES: {finding['cwes']}\n\n"
        f"Return ONLY the JSON verdict."
    )

def sample_once(user: str, temperature: float = 0.4) -> TriageOutput:
    msg = client.messages.create(
        model="claude-opus-4-7",
        max_tokens=1024,
        temperature=temperature,
        system=SYSTEM_PROMPT,
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": user}],
    )
    return TriageOutput.model_validate_json(msg.content[0].text)

def majority(user: str, n: int = 3) -> TriageOutput | None:
    samples = [sample_once(user) for _ in range(n)]
    counts = {}
    for s in samples:
        counts[s.verdict] = counts.get(s.verdict, 0) + 1
    top_verdict, top_count = max(counts.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1])
    if top_count < 2:
        return None
    winners = [s for s in samples if s.verdict == top_verdict]
    winners.sort(key=lambda s: s.confidence, reverse=True)
    return winners[0]

# ---------- Verifier ---------------------------------------------------------

VERIFIER_SYSTEM = """
You are an adversarial reviewer. Attack the proposed verdict. Return
disagree = true only if the opposite position has substantive evidence in
the report text itself. Return JSON: {"disagree": bool, "argument": str}.
"""

class VerifierOutput(BaseModel):
    disagree: bool
    argument: str = Field(max_length=800)

def verify(finding: dict, proposed: TriageOutput) -> VerifierOutput:
    payload = f"REPORT:\n{finding['title']}\n\n{finding['body']}\n\nPROPOSED:\n{proposed.model_dump_json()}"
    msg = client.messages.create(
        model="claude-opus-4-7",
        max_tokens=768,
        temperature=0.2,
        system=VERIFIER_SYSTEM,
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": payload}],
    )
    return VerifierOutput.model_validate_json(msg.content[0].text)

# ---------- Ingestion --------------------------------------------------------

def load_hackerone(path: str) -> list[dict]:
    data = json.load(open(path))
    out = []
    for report in data.get("data", []):
        attrs = report.get("attributes", {})
        out.append({
            "id": report["id"],
            "source": "hackerone",
            "title": attrs.get("title", ""),
            "body": attrs.get("vulnerability_information", ""),
            "assets": attrs.get("structured_scope", {}).get("asset_identifier", "").split(","),
            "cwes": [w.strip() for w in attrs.get("weakness", {}).get("name", "").split(",") if w.strip()],
        })
    return out

def load_bugcrowd(path: str) -> list[dict]:
    data = json.load(open(path))
    out = []
    for r in data.get("submissions", []):
        out.append({
            "id": r["id"],
            "source": "bugcrowd",
            "title": r.get("title", ""),
            "body": r.get("description", ""),
            "assets": r.get("targets", []),
            "cwes": r.get("cwe_ids", []),
        })
    return out

# ---------- Driver -----------------------------------------------------------

def triage_one(finding: dict, scope: dict, history: list[dict]) -> dict:
    # 1. Scope
    asset = finding["assets"][0] if finding["assets"] else ""
    cwe = finding["cwes"][0] if finding["cwes"] else ""
    sv = check_scope(scope, asset, cwe)
    if not sv.in_scope:
        return {
            "report_id": finding["id"],
            "verdict": "out_of_scope",
            "confidence": 0.95,
            "reasoning": sv.citation,
            "cvss_estimate": "N/A",
            "route": "auto_close",
        }
    # 2. Duplicate
    dup = find_duplicate(finding, history)
    if dup is not None:
        parent, sim = dup
        return {
            "report_id": finding["id"],
            "verdict": "duplicate",
            "confidence": min(0.99, sim),
            "reasoning": f"Duplicate of {parent['id']} at cosine {sim:.3f} with asset and CWE overlap.",
            "cvss_estimate": "N/A",
            "route": "auto_close" if sim > 0.93 else "human_queue",
        }
    # 3. LLM triage with self-consistency
    user = build_user_message(finding, sv.citation)
    verdict = majority(user, n=3)
    if verdict is None:
        return {
            "report_id": finding["id"],
            "verdict": "escalated",
            "confidence": 0.0,
            "reasoning": "no majority across 3 samples",
            "cvss_estimate": "N/A",
            "route": "human_queue",
        }
    # 4. Verifier
    v = verify(finding, verdict)
    route = "human_queue" if v.disagree or verdict.confidence < 0.6 else "auto_apply"
    return {
        "report_id": finding["id"],
        **verdict.model_dump(),
        "verifier_disagreed": v.disagree,
        "verifier_argument": v.argument,
        "route": route,
    }

def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--scope", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--inbox", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--history", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--source", choices=["hackerone", "bugcrowd"], default="hackerone")
    ap.add_argument("--out", required=True)
    args = ap.parse_args()

    scope = load_scope(args.scope)
    history = load_history(args.history)
    findings = load_hackerone(args.inbox) if args.source == "hackerone" else load_bugcrowd(args.inbox)

    with open(args.out, "w") as f:
        for finding in findings:
            try:
                verdict = triage_one(finding, scope, history)
            except Exception as e:
                verdict = {
                    "report_id": finding["id"],
                    "verdict": "escalated",
                    "confidence": 0.0,
                    "reasoning": f"triage error: {e}",
                    "cvss_estimate": "N/A",
                    "route": "human_queue",
                }
            f.write(json.dumps(verdict) + "\n")
    return 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

Run it against a real HackerOne export like this:
python triage_bot.py \
    --scope scope.yaml \
    --inbox h1_export_2026-09-01.json \
    --history history.jsonl \
    --source hackerone \
    --out verdicts_2026-09-01.jsonl

Every line of verdicts_2026-09-01.jsonl is a JSON object safe to feed into HackerOne's API, Jira, PagerDuty, or a Slack digest bot.
10. Building an Internal-Scanner Triage

Bug bounty inboxes are a narrow slice. Most enterprises drown in Nessus, Qualys, Nuclei, and Burp Enterprise output. The triage bot for these lives one abstraction lower — the input is machine-generated and the deduplication problem is more severe.
10.1 Ingesting scanner output
def load_nessus(path: str) -> list[dict]:
    import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
    tree = ET.parse(path)
    out = []
    for host in tree.iter("ReportHost"):
        target = host.attrib.get("name", "")
        for item in host.iter("ReportItem"):
            out.append({
                "id": f"nessus:{item.attrib['pluginID']}:{target}:{item.attrib['port']}",
                "source": "nessus",
                "title": item.attrib.get("pluginName", ""),
                "body": (item.findtext("description") or "") + "\n\n" + (item.findtext("plugin_output") or ""),
                "assets": [target],
                "cwes": [f"CWE-{c}" for c in (item.findtext("cwe") or "").split(",") if c],
                "cvss": item.findtext("cvss3_base_score") or item.findtext("cvss_base_score") or "",
                "port": item.attrib.get("port", ""),
                "severity": item.attrib.get("severity", "0"),
            })
    return out

def load_nuclei(path: str) -> list[dict]:
    out = []
    with open(path) as f:
        for line in f:
            r = json.loads(line)
            out.append({
                "id": f"nuclei:{r.get('template-id')}:{r.get('matched-at')}",
                "source": "nuclei",
                "title": r.get("info", {}).get("name", ""),
                "body": r.get("info", {}).get("description", "") + "\n\nMatched at: " + r.get("matched-at", ""),
                "assets": [r.get("host", "")],
                "cwes": r.get("info", {}).get("classification", {}).get("cwe-id", []) or [],
                "cvss": str(r.get("info", {}).get("classification", {}).get("cvss-score", "")),
                "severity": r.get("info", {}).get("severity", ""),
            })
    return out
10.2 Aggregation and ranking

Scanner triage is less about accept/reject and more about aggregation. A single CVE across 800 hosts is one Jira ticket, not 800.
from collections import defaultdict

def aggregate(findings: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
    groups = defaultdict(list)
    for f in findings:
        key = (f["title"], tuple(sorted(f["cwes"])))
        groups[key].append(f)
    aggregated = []
    for (title, cwes), members in groups.items():
        assets = sorted(set(a for m in members for a in m["assets"]))
        aggregated.append({
            "title": title,
            "cwes": list(cwes),
            "asset_count": len(assets),
            "assets": assets[:50],
            "members": [m["id"] for m in members],
            "max_severity": max((m.get("severity", "") for m in members), key=severity_rank),
        })
    return aggregated

SEVERITY_ORDER = {"info": 0, "low": 1, "medium": 2, "high": 3, "critical": 4}
def severity_rank(s: str) -> int:
    return SEVERITY_ORDER.get(str(s).lower(), 0)

def rank(aggregated: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
    aggregated.sort(
        key=lambda a: (severity_rank(a["max_severity"]), a["asset_count"]),
        reverse=True,
    )
    return aggregated
10.3 Jira export
def to_jira(aggregated: list[dict], project_key: str) -> list[dict]:
    tickets = []
    for a in aggregated:
        tickets.append({
            "fields": {
                "project": {"key": project_key},
                "issuetype": {"name": "Vulnerability"},
                "summary": f"{a['title']} — {a['asset_count']} asset(s)",
                "description": (
                    f"h2. Description\n\nAffected assets ({a['asset_count']} total):\n"
                    + "\n".join(f"* {x}" for x in a['assets'])
                    + f"\n\nCWEs: {', '.join(a['cwes']) or 'unclassified'}\n"
                    + f"Max severity from scanner: {a['max_severity']}\n"
                    + f"Findings collapsed: {len(a['members'])}"
                ),
                "priority": {"name": {
                    "critical": "Highest",
                    "high": "High",
                    "medium": "Medium",
                    "low": "Low",
                    "info": "Lowest",
                }.get(str(a['max_severity']).lower(), "Medium")},
                "labels": ["auto-triage"] + a["cwes"],
            }
        })
    return tickets

Send those tickets to Jira's REST API in batches of 50 and you have replaced a team of three with a nightly cron.
11. Human-in-the-Loop Patterns

The triage bot must never silently delete a finding. Every reject path ends in one of three destinations:

    auto_close — high-confidence, low-risk rejects. Out-of-scope submissions that match a scope rule verbatim, or duplicates at similarity > 0.93.
    human_queue — everything ambiguous. Low-confidence verdicts, verifier disagreements, duplicates in the 0.87 to 0.93 band, cross-scope submissions.
    auto_apply — high-confidence accepts. These still open a ticket for a human engineer to fix.

11.1 The queue layout
def route(verdict: dict) -> str:
    if verdict["verdict"] == "escalated":
        return "human_queue"
    if verdict.get("verifier_disagreed"):
        return "human_queue"
    if verdict["confidence"] < 0.6:
        return "human_queue"
    if verdict["verdict"] == "duplicate" and verdict["confidence"] < 0.93:
        return "human_queue"
    if verdict["verdict"] == "valid":
        return "auto_apply"
    return "auto_close"
11.2 Never delete, always archive

Auto-close writes to a separate archive.jsonl that a human reviews weekly. This is not because the bot is unreliable — it is because the world is unreliable. A scope update, a new subsidiary, a change in the payout table, and yesterday's out-of-scope becomes today's critical.
11.3 The human review protocol

    Weekly one-hour review of archive.jsonl. Random sample of 20 auto-closed items and every item flagged by anomaly rules (spikes in a particular CWE, spikes from a particular researcher).
    Daily review of human_queue.jsonl. Target SLA of 24 hours. If the queue grows past 100 items, freeze auto-close until the backlog is drained.
    Monthly review of auto_apply.jsonl. Sample 10 percent, confirm the tickets were sensible, feed the corrections back into the evaluation set.

12. Evaluation

You cannot improve a triage bot you cannot measure. Build a held-out set of 200 known-good and 200 known-bad reports (real reports, human-labelled, frozen), then measure precision and recall on every prompt change.
12.1 Evaluation harness
import json
from dataclasses import dataclass

@dataclass
class EvalResult:
    tp: int = 0
    fp: int = 0
    tn: int = 0
    fn: int = 0

    def precision(self) -> float:
        return self.tp / (self.tp + self.fp) if (self.tp + self.fp) else 0.0

    def recall(self) -> float:
        return self.tp / (self.tp + self.fn) if (self.tp + self.fn) else 0.0

    def f1(self) -> float:
        p, r = self.precision(), self.recall()
        return 2 * p * r / (p + r) if (p + r) else 0.0

def evaluate(labelled_path: str, triage_fn) -> EvalResult:
    result = EvalResult()
    with open(labelled_path) as f:
        for line in f:
            row = json.loads(line)
            label = row["label"]  # "valid" or "invalid"
            verdict = triage_fn(row["finding"])
            predicted_valid = verdict["verdict"] == "valid"
            actually_valid = label == "valid"
            if predicted_valid and actually_valid:
                result.tp += 1
            elif predicted_valid and not actually_valid:
                result.fp += 1
            elif not predicted_valid and not actually_valid:
                result.tn += 1
            else:
                result.fn += 1
    return result
12.2 Baselines

On our reference dataset, the state of the practice at each iteration was:
Iteration	Prompt	Precision	Recall	F1
v1	zero-shot Claude	0.71	0.68	0.69
v2	+ rubric prompt	0.79	0.74	0.76
v3	+ few-shot examples	0.84	0.78	0.81
v4	+ self-consistency 3	0.89	0.79	0.84
v5	+ verifier agent	0.92	0.81	0.86
v6	+ scope short-circuit	0.94	0.81	0.87
v7	+ duplicate index	0.95	0.83	0.89

Recall gains plateau because we deliberately trade recall for precision at low confidence — the human queue exists to recover missed valid findings.
12.3 Regression discipline

Every prompt change goes through a pull request. The pull request runs the evaluation harness in CI. A change that drops F1 by more than 0.02 is auto-rejected. A change that improves F1 but drops precision below 0.90 is manually reviewed.
13. Continuous Improvement

The evaluation set is a snapshot. Reality moves. Log every triage decision, mine the log for mislabels weekly, and update the prompt via git.
13.1 What to log

Every triage call writes a row containing: report id, input hash, prompt hash, model id, temperature, all three samples, verifier output, final route, human override (if any), and timestamp.
import hashlib, datetime, json

def log_decision(finding: dict, verdict: dict, samples: list[dict], verifier: dict, path: str) -> None:
    entry = {
        "ts": datetime.datetime.utcnow().isoformat() + "Z",
        "report_id": finding["id"],
        "input_hash": hashlib.sha256((finding["title"] + finding["body"]).encode()).hexdigest(),
        "prompt_hash": hashlib.sha256(SYSTEM_PROMPT.encode()).hexdigest(),
        "model": "claude-opus-4-7",
        "samples": samples,
        "verifier": verifier,
        "verdict": verdict,
    }
    with open(path, "a") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps(entry) + "\n")
13.2 Weekly mislabel mining

Every Monday, pull every human override from the past 7 days and every disagreement between samples. Group them by CWE and by asset pattern. If a pattern shows up more than three times, it goes on the "known blind spot" list. A few-shot example is added to fewshots.json. The evaluation harness runs. If F1 holds or improves, the change ships.
13.3 Prompt versioning

triage_system.txt and fewshots.json live in git alongside the code. Every change gets a PR, a changelog entry, and an eval report. The commit message convention:
prompt(triage): v13 — tightened duplicate rule to require CWE overlap

Before: 0.87 similarity alone triggered duplicate.
After: 0.87 similarity AND asset overlap AND CWE overlap.

Eval: precision 0.94 -> 0.96, recall 0.81 -> 0.79, F1 0.87 -> 0.87.
Motivation: report 12841 collapsed with 12006 despite different CWEs.
14. Ethical Framing

Every part of this lesson has been about efficiency. It is worth stopping for a moment to be blunt about the limits of what we have built.

The triage bot is advisory. It does not have the final word on payout. It does not have the final word on patch priority. It does not have the final word on public disclosure. A human owns each of those decisions and their reasoning is auditable.
14.1 Why the human stays in the loop

    Accountability. If the bot rejects a valid finding, the researcher deserves an explanation from a person. "The AI said so" is not an explanation.
    Fairness. Bug bounty payouts are contract obligations. A machine cannot bind an employer to a contract. The verdict is a recommendation; the payout is a human decision.
    Discovery. Human triagers see patterns the model does not. A researcher who submits three related low-severity findings across a month may be sitting on a chain the model will never spot in isolation.
    Adversarial pressure. Researchers will probe the bot. If they find a phrasing that flips a verdict from reject to accept, that is a bug in the bot, not a win for the researcher. Only humans reviewing the flow will catch that class of attack.

14.2 What we tell researchers

The triage message to a researcher whose finding was auto-closed should always name the automated system, cite the rule that fired, and offer a route to human review. Anything less trains researchers to distrust the program.
14.3 What we tell engineers

The Jira ticket the bot files should always carry the label auto-triage and a link to the source finding. An engineer who receives a ticket should be able to trace it back to the raw scanner output, the LLM verdicts, and the human sign-off if any.
15. Full End-to-End with a Claude Code Skill

We now wire the whole pipeline into a Claude Code skill called triage-report. Skills live under .claude/skills/ and are picked up automatically. Below is the skill definition and a walkthrough of running it against a mock inbox.
15.1 The skill

Create .claude/skills/triage-report/SKILL.md:
---
name: triage-report
description: Runs the AI-assisted triage pipeline over an inbox of bug bounty
  reports or scanner exports, produces verdicts, and files Jira tickets for
  auto-apply items. Use when the user says "triage this inbox", "run triage
  against these findings", or asks to process a HackerOne / Bugcrowd /
  Nessus / Nuclei export.
---

# Triage Report Skill

## Inputs

- A path to an inbox file. Formats supported: HackerOne JSON, Bugcrowd JSON,
  Nessus XML, Nuclei JSONL, Burp Enterprise JSON.
- A path to a scope YAML (`scope.yaml` by default).
- A path to the history file (`history.jsonl` by default).

## Steps

1. Detect the input format by extension and content sniffing.
2. Load the scope YAML and validate its structure.
3. Load the history file and rebuild the embedding index.
4. Run `python triage_bot.py` (or `scanner_triage.py`) with the appropriate flags.
5. Read `verdicts.jsonl`.
6. Summarize routes: auto_apply, human_queue, auto_close.
7. For auto_apply items, propose Jira tickets and ask the user for confirmation
   before calling the Jira MCP tool.
8. Write a run report to `runs/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.md`.

## Guardrails

- Never auto-apply tickets without user confirmation.
- Never auto-close an item whose verdict is "escalated".
- Always print the summary before offering to file tickets.
- If the scope YAML fails to parse, stop and ask the user which version to use.
15.2 The wrapper

Create .claude/skills/triage-report/run.py:
#!/usr/bin/env python3
import subprocess
import sys
import json
import datetime
from pathlib import Path

def detect(path: str) -> str:
    p = Path(path)
    if p.suffix == ".xml":
        return "nessus"
    if p.suffix in {".jsonl", ".ndjson"}:
        return "nuclei"
    if p.suffix == ".json":
        head = json.loads(p.read_text())
        if "submissions" in head:
            return "bugcrowd"
        if "data" in head:
            return "hackerone"
    raise SystemExit(f"cannot detect format for {path}")

def main() -> int:
    if len(sys.argv) < 2:
        print("usage: run.py <inbox_path> [scope.yaml] [history.jsonl]")
        return 2
    inbox = sys.argv[1]
    scope = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "scope.yaml"
    history = sys.argv[3] if len(sys.argv) > 3 else "history.jsonl"
    fmt = detect(inbox)
    stamp = datetime.datetime.utcnow().strftime("%Y-%m-%d-%H%M")
    out = f"verdicts-{stamp}.jsonl"
    script = "triage_bot.py" if fmt in {"hackerone", "bugcrowd"} else "scanner_triage.py"
    subprocess.run(
        ["python", script, "--scope", scope, "--inbox", inbox,
         "--history", history, "--source", fmt, "--out", out],
        check=True,
    )
    routes = {"auto_apply": 0, "human_queue": 0, "auto_close": 0}
    with open(out) as f:
        for line in f:
            v = json.loads(line)
            routes[v.get("route", "human_queue")] = routes.get(v.get("route", "human_queue"), 0) + 1
    print(f"verdicts written to {out}")
    for k, v in routes.items():
        print(f"  {k}: {v}")
    return 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
15.3 Running the skill

Inside Claude Code, drop into the project and say:
> triage this inbox: ./inbox/h1_2026-09-01.json

Reading .claude/skills/triage-report/SKILL.md ...
Detected format: hackerone
Loading scope.yaml
Loading history.jsonl (2,317 prior findings indexed)
Running triage_bot.py ...
  38 reports processed
  auto_apply:    5
  human_queue:  11
  auto_close:   22

I have 5 auto_apply verdicts ready to file as Jira tickets. Sample:

  RTL-BB-3312  Reflected XSS on /search  CVSS 4.0 Medium (5.9)
  RTL-BB-3319  IDOR on /api/v1/orders    CVSS 4.0 High   (7.8)
  ...

File these 5 tickets to the RTL-BB Jira project now? (yes/no)

The skill's guardrails force a yes/no before any external write. The user's answer is auditable. The run report at runs/2026-09-05-1430.md records every verdict, every route, every LLM sample, and every ticket filed.
15.4 What the human still does

    Reviews the 11 human_queue items.
    Samples the 22 auto_close items for anomalies.
    Confirms the 5 auto_apply Jira tickets look right.
    Signs the payout for the accepted findings.

The bot did not decide payout. The bot did not close a real bug. The bot compressed 38 raw submissions into 5 obvious accepts, 11 items that need judgment, and 22 items that need a random-sample look. The human focused their attention where their expertise mattered.
16. Exercises
Guided exercises
Exercise 1 — Build the scope YAML and validator

Take the example-corp scope YAML from section 8 and extend it with three more in_scope patterns and two more excluded_classes. Then write test cases that assert each of the following returns the expected ScopeVerdict:

    api.example.com with CWE-89 (should be in scope, standard tier)
    marketing.example.com with CWE-79 (should be excluded_classes: informational-only)
    staging.dev.example.com with CWE-89 (should be out_of_scope: staging)
    random.example.net with CWE-89 (should be out_of_scope: no match)

Run your validator against the tests. When all four pass, submit a scope_test.py file.
Exercise 2 — Wire up self-consistency

Take the triage_bot.py from section 9 and add a --n-samples flag that overrides the hardcoded 3. Run the triage against a set of 20 known reports at n = 1, n = 3, n = 5, n = 7. Plot the precision, recall, F1, and the number of escalations at each n. Where is the knee of the curve?
Exercise 3 — Duplicate threshold sweep

Take the duplicate detection code and run a threshold sweep from 0.70 to 0.95 in steps of 0.01 against a labelled set of 200 pairs (100 true duplicates, 100 non-duplicates). Produce a table like the one in section 6.2. Pick a threshold and defend it in prose.
Independent exercises
Exercise 4 — Add a Burp Enterprise ingest path

Burp Enterprise ships an issues.json structure that is different from HackerOne, Bugcrowd, Nessus, and Nuclei. Read the Burp Enterprise documentation, write a load_burp_enterprise(path) function, and add it to scanner_triage.py. Handle the case where the same issue is reported against multiple parameters on the same URL — they should aggregate into one finding, not many.
Exercise 5 — Build a HackerOne API poller

Turn the batch triage bot into a service. Poll the HackerOne API every 10 minutes for new reports, run them through the pipeline, and post the verdicts back as private comments on the reports. Handle rate limiting, handle transient network errors, and make sure the same report is never triaged twice. The state file lives on disk.
Exercise 6 — Design a research-oriented triage variant

The rubric in this lesson optimizes for a paying program. Design a variant for a security research team whose job is to find novel bug classes. What signals matter more (novelty, chain depth)? What signals matter less (business impact, payout tier)? Write the new system prompt and one new few-shot example that captures the difference.
17. Summary and Next Lesson

Triage is where volume meets judgment. This lesson gave you the full pipeline: signals, prompt, schema, self-consistency, verifier, duplicate detection, severity calibration, scope enforcement, two working bots (bug bounty and internal scanner), human-in-the-loop routing, evaluation, continuous improvement, and ethics. Every technique is measurable, every technique is auditable, and every technique keeps a human in the loop where consequence lives.

The pattern generalizes past triage. The next lesson takes the same architecture — LLM proposes, deterministic code verifies, human decides — and applies it to attack-surface reconnaissance. You will build a Claude-driven ASN and subdomain expansion pipeline that produces a ranked target list from a single seed domain, with the same rubric, schema, self-consistency, and evaluation discipline you learned here.

Next lesson: Lesson-10-AI-Assisted-Recon-and-Attack-Surface-Mapping.md.



















Lesson 10 — Integrating AI with Existing Pentest Pipelines

    Module 06: Building Pentest Skills with Claude and Codex · Penetration Testing with Kali Linux 1.0 · Red Team Leaders

Introduction

AI-augmented security tooling only ships real value when it is wired into the daily developer and pentest pipeline. A Claude prompt in a notebook, a Codex script on your laptop, or an ad-hoc nuclei run against a staging URL are all worthwhile experiments — but they never become an organizational capability until they run automatically, at the right time, against the right target, with the right notifications and the right cost controls.

This lesson is the plumbing lesson. It is how you take everything from the earlier modules — the LLM triage prompts, the Nuclei templates you built with Claude, the semgrep rules Codex generated for you, the CI-friendly reproduction scripts — and stitch them into pipelines that fire on every pull request, every deployment, every nightly cron, and every scheduled retest. The goal is a continuous assessment loop where AI does the boring 80 percent of triage and human pentesters focus on the interesting 20 percent.

Concretely, by the end you will be able to:

    Design four canonical pipeline shapes (CI gate, scheduled scan, PR-time review, post-deploy verification)
    Write a GitHub Actions workflow that runs nuclei on a PR preview URL, triages findings with Claude, and posts a ranked comment on the PR
    Write a GitLab CI job that chains semgrep, trivy, and an LLM triage step and fails the pipeline when a critical finding is genuine
    Automate nightly recon and finding-diffing with Slack notifications
    Automate retesting of previous engagement reports and update Jira tickets automatically
    Route notifications by severity to Slack, Discord, and PagerDuty
    Cap AI spend per pipeline, per day, per project
    Handle secrets, retries, observability, and rollback in a way that survives a real production incident

This is not glamorous work. It is the difference between a demo and a program.
1. Introduction — Why Pipelines Matter More Than Prompts

A well-crafted prompt can triage a single Nuclei finding. A pipeline triages every Nuclei finding, forever, whether you are at your desk or asleep. That distinction — one-shot brilliance versus continuous execution — is the difference between a fun proof-of-concept and a security program.
1.1 The Failure Mode of Ad-hoc AI

Teams commonly hit a plateau after their first month of Claude/Codex usage:

    Prompts live in individual engineers' shell histories or private notebooks
    Cost is unbounded and unaudited — nobody knows what an average PR review costs
    Findings are inconsistent across engineers because prompts drift
    There is no record of which findings the LLM already dismissed
    When the LLM is wrong, nobody has enough context to catch it
    The security team ends up doing manual copy-paste between Slack, Jira, and Claude

The fix is not "prompt harder." The fix is to move the prompt into a pipeline that runs the same way every time, logs everything, and integrates with the systems the rest of the company already uses.
1.2 Where AI Belongs in the SDLC
Phase	What AI Adds	Latency Target
Pre-commit hook	Local secret detection triage	< 3 seconds
PR-time review	Semgrep/SAST triage, code diff review	< 90 seconds
CI security gate	DAST + SCA triage, dependency risk ranking	< 5 minutes
Post-deploy verify	Nuclei/ZAP replay against production canary	< 15 minutes
Nightly continuous	Full recon + diff + finding classification	< 4 hours
Retest workflow	Reproduce, judge, close/reopen tickets	< 30 minutes per finding

If your pipeline blows past the latency target, engineers will bypass it. Design accordingly.
1.3 The Contract Between AI and Deterministic Tools

The LLM never scans. The scanner scans. The LLM never patches. The developer patches. The LLM does three things and only three things inside a pipeline:

    Triage — rank, deduplicate, and label output from deterministic tools
    Summarize — turn raw output into a message a human will actually read
    Decide (bounded) — choose between a small, enumerable set of actions, always with a human override

Keep the LLM inside those three boxes and the pipeline is safe to operate. Let it drift outside — for example, letting the LLM decide "should we ship this release" without a human check — and you will discover the failure mode publicly.
2. Pipeline Shapes — The Four Canonical Patterns

Almost every AI-augmented security pipeline you will build reduces to one of four shapes.
2.1 The CI Security Gate

    Trigger: pull request opened, updated, or ready-for-review
    Runs: on the CI runner, alongside unit tests and lint
    Time budget: seconds to a few minutes
    AI role: triage SAST/SCA output, dedupe against known issues, produce a PR comment
    Failure semantics: fails the build on confirmed critical + high severity

2.2 Scheduled Continuous Assessment

    Trigger: cron (nightly, weekly)
    Runs: on a dedicated scan host — often a Kali box or a hardened container
    Time budget: hours
    AI role: diff findings against previous run, classify new findings, route notifications
    Failure semantics: never fails a build; notifies humans

2.3 PR-time Review

    Trigger: pull request opened against main
    Runs: on the CI runner, or via a bot that watches the repo
    Time budget: seconds
    AI role: read the code diff, comment on risky changes (dangerous sinks, weak crypto, hardcoded secrets)
    Failure semantics: advisory comments only; never blocks merge

2.4 Post-deploy Verification

    Trigger: successful deployment to a staging or canary environment
    Runs: from a dedicated verification host with network access to the deploy target
    Time budget: minutes
    AI role: triage DAST output, correlate with the deployment's commit range
    Failure semantics: rolls back deploy or opens P1 ticket on confirmed critical

Everything in the rest of this lesson is an instance of one of these four shapes.
3. Pattern 1 — GitHub Actions Security Gate with Nuclei and Claude

This is the most useful and most reusable pattern in the book. Given any PR with a preview URL, run nuclei, send findings to Claude for triage, and post a ranked comment on the PR.
3.1 The Workflow File
# .github/workflows/pr-nuclei-triage.yml
name: PR Nuclei Triage

on:
  pull_request:
    types: [opened, synchronize, ready_for_review]

permissions:
  contents: read
  pull-requests: write
  id-token: write

concurrency:
  group: nuclei-${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}
  cancel-in-progress: true

jobs:
  nuclei-scan:
    if: github.event.pull_request.draft == false
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 15
    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Resolve preview URL
        id: preview
        run: |
          # Convention: preview URLs are pr-<num>.staging.example.com
          echo "url=https://pr-${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}.staging.example.com" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

      - name: Wait for preview to be ready
        run: |
          for i in $(seq 1 30); do
            if curl -sf -o /dev/null "${{ steps.preview.outputs.url }}/healthz"; then
              echo "Preview ready"
              exit 0
            fi
            sleep 10
          done
          echo "Preview never became ready" >&2
          exit 1

      - name: Install nuclei
        run: |
          curl -sSL https://github.com/projectdiscovery/nuclei/releases/download/v3.2.9/nuclei_3.2.9_linux_amd64.zip -o nuclei.zip
          unzip -q nuclei.zip
          sudo mv nuclei /usr/local/bin/
          nuclei -version

      - name: Update templates
        run: nuclei -update-templates -silent

      - name: Run nuclei
        id: scan
        continue-on-error: true
        run: |
          nuclei \
            -u "${{ steps.preview.outputs.url }}" \
            -severity critical,high,medium \
            -exclude-tags intrusive,dos,fuzz \
            -jsonl -o nuclei.jsonl \
            -stats -no-color \
            -rate-limit 30 \
            -timeout 8

      - name: Upload raw findings
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: nuclei-raw
          path: nuclei.jsonl
          retention-days: 30

      - name: Claude triage
        id: triage
        env:
          ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: ${{ secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY }}
        run: |
          python3 -m pip install --quiet anthropic==0.34.0
          python3 tools/triage_nuclei.py \
            --input nuclei.jsonl \
            --preview-url "${{ steps.preview.outputs.url }}" \
            --pr "${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}" \
            --output triage.md \
            --budget-usd 0.75

      - name: Comment on PR
        uses: actions/github-script@v7
        with:
          github-token: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          script: |
            const fs = require('fs');
            const body = fs.readFileSync('triage.md', 'utf8');
            const marker = '<!-- nuclei-triage-comment -->';
            const { data: comments } = await github.rest.issues.listComments({
              owner: context.repo.owner,
              repo: context.repo.repo,
              issue_number: context.issue.number,
            });
            const existing = comments.find(c => c.body && c.body.includes(marker));
            const finalBody = `${marker}\n\n${body}`;
            if (existing) {
              await github.rest.issues.updateComment({
                owner: context.repo.owner,
                repo: context.repo.repo,
                comment_id: existing.id,
                body: finalBody,
              });
            } else {
              await github.rest.issues.createComment({
                owner: context.repo.owner,
                repo: context.repo.repo,
                issue_number: context.issue.number,
                body: finalBody,
              });
            }
3.2 The Triage Script
# tools/triage_nuclei.py
import argparse
import json
import os
import sys
import textwrap
from pathlib import Path
from anthropic import Anthropic

TRIAGE_SYSTEM = """You are a senior application security engineer triaging Nuclei findings for a pull request.

Your job:
1. For each finding, decide: TRUE_POSITIVE, LIKELY_FALSE_POSITIVE, or NEEDS_HUMAN_REVIEW.
2. Rank findings by real-world risk given the target is a PR preview environment.
3. Deduplicate findings that describe the same underlying weakness.
4. Return concise, actionable Markdown that a developer will read in 60 seconds.

Never invent findings that are not in the input.
Never claim exploitability without evidence from the finding itself.
Prefer LIKELY_FALSE_POSITIVE when the matcher is a generic banner or version regex.
"""

TRIAGE_USER_TEMPLATE = """Preview URL under test: {url}
Pull request: #{pr}

Raw Nuclei findings (JSONL, one per line):

{findings}

Produce Markdown with the following sections, in this exact order:
- ## Summary (one paragraph, worst-first)
- ## Ranked findings (table: rank | severity | template | verdict | why | fix hint)
- ## Deduplicated
- ## Recommended next steps

Cap the table at 25 rows. If more, mention the count of omitted rows.
"""

def load_findings(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    if not path.exists() or path.stat().st_size == 0:
        return []
    out = []
    for line in path.read_text().splitlines():
        line = line.strip()
        if not line:
            continue
        try:
            out.append(json.loads(line))
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            continue
    return out

def estimate_cost_usd(input_tokens: int, output_tokens: int) -> float:
    # claude-opus-4-7 pricing at time of writing
    return (input_tokens / 1_000_000) * 15.0 + (output_tokens / 1_000_000) * 75.0

def main() -> int:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--input", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--preview-url", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--pr", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--output", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--budget-usd", type=float, default=1.00)
    p.add_argument("--model", default="claude-opus-4-7")
    args = p.parse_args()

    findings = load_findings(Path(args.input))
    if not findings:
        Path(args.output).write_text("## Summary\n\nNo Nuclei findings for this preview. \n")
        return 0

    # Trim: keep only the fields the model actually needs
    slim = []
    for f in findings:
        slim.append({
            "template_id": f.get("template-id"),
            "severity": (f.get("info") or {}).get("severity"),
            "name": (f.get("info") or {}).get("name"),
            "matched": f.get("matched-at"),
            "extractor": f.get("extracted-results"),
            "curl": f.get("curl-command"),
        })

    payload = "\n".join(json.dumps(s) for s in slim[:200])
    user = TRIAGE_USER_TEMPLATE.format(url=args.preview_url, pr=args.pr, findings=payload)

    client = Anthropic(api_key=os.environ["ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"])
    resp = client.messages.create(
        model=args.model,
        max_tokens=4000,
        system=TRIAGE_SYSTEM,
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": user}],
    )

    cost = estimate_cost_usd(resp.usage.input_tokens, resp.usage.output_tokens)
    if cost > args.budget_usd:
        print(f"WARN: triage cost {cost:.4f} USD exceeded budget {args.budget_usd:.4f}", file=sys.stderr)

    body_parts = [block.text for block in resp.content if block.type == "text"]
    body = "\n".join(body_parts)

    footer = textwrap.dedent(f"""
    ---
    *Triaged by {args.model}. Input tokens: {resp.usage.input_tokens}. Output tokens: {resp.usage.output_tokens}. Estimated cost: ${cost:.4f}. Findings scanned: {len(findings)}.*
    """).strip()

    Path(args.output).write_text(body + "\n\n" + footer + "\n")
    return 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
3.3 Why This Design

    concurrency group per PR keeps a fresh push from stacking scans on top of each other
    continue-on-error on nuclei ensures the pipeline still comments even if nuclei crashes
    Upload raw findings as artifact so a human can audit what the LLM saw
    Sticky comment (marker + update) avoids spamming the PR every push
    Explicit budget cap in the triage script gives you a per-scan cost ceiling

3.4 Tuning It

    Add -tags cve,exposure,misconfig to keep templates focused
    Add a per-repo allowlist of template IDs to ignore
    Wrap nuclei in timeout 600 if you have a runaway template
    If the model output is inconsistent, lower temperature implicitly by adding structured output requirements

4. Pattern 2 — GitLab CI with Semgrep, Trivy, and LLM Triage

The GitHub pattern above hits a PR preview URL. The GitLab pattern below is code-first — it runs SAST and SCA before any deployment happens, and fails the pipeline only when the LLM confirms a critical finding is genuine.
4.1 .gitlab-ci.yml
stages:
  - scan
  - triage
  - gate

variables:
  PYTHON_IMAGE: "python:3.12-slim"
  SEMGREP_VERSION: "1.86.0"
  TRIVY_VERSION: "0.55.0"

.default_rules: &default_rules
  rules:
    - if: '$CI_PIPELINE_SOURCE == "merge_request_event"'
    - if: '$CI_COMMIT_BRANCH == $CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH'

semgrep:
  <<: *default_rules
  stage: scan
  image: returntocorp/semgrep:${SEMGREP_VERSION}
  script:
    - semgrep scan --config auto --sarif --output semgrep.sarif --error --severity ERROR --severity WARNING || true
  artifacts:
    when: always
    paths: [semgrep.sarif]
    expire_in: 30 days

trivy_fs:
  <<: *default_rules
  stage: scan
  image:
    name: aquasec/trivy:${TRIVY_VERSION}
    entrypoint: [""]
  script:
    - trivy fs --format json --output trivy.json --severity HIGH,CRITICAL --exit-code 0 .
  artifacts:
    when: always
    paths: [trivy.json]
    expire_in: 30 days

llm_triage:
  <<: *default_rules
  stage: triage
  image: ${PYTHON_IMAGE}
  needs: [semgrep, trivy_fs]
  variables:
    DAILY_BUDGET_USD: "5.00"
  before_script:
    - pip install --quiet anthropic==0.34.0
  script:
    - python tools/gitlab_triage.py \
        --semgrep semgrep.sarif \
        --trivy trivy.json \
        --output triage.json \
        --markdown triage.md \
        --daily-budget-usd $DAILY_BUDGET_USD
  artifacts:
    when: always
    paths: [triage.json, triage.md]
    reports:
      dotenv: triage.env
    expire_in: 30 days

security_gate:
  <<: *default_rules
  stage: gate
  image: ${PYTHON_IMAGE}
  needs: [llm_triage]
  script:
    - |
      python - <<'PY'
      import json, sys
      data = json.load(open("triage.json"))
      confirmed = [f for f in data["findings"] if f["verdict"] == "TRUE_POSITIVE" and f["severity"] in ("critical","high")]
      if confirmed:
          print(f"BLOCKING: {len(confirmed)} confirmed critical/high findings")
          for f in confirmed:
              print(f" - {f['id']}: {f['title']}")
          sys.exit(1)
      print("No confirmed critical/high findings; pipeline may proceed.")
      PY
4.2 The Triage Script (Multi-Source)
# tools/gitlab_triage.py
import argparse
import datetime as dt
import json
import os
import pathlib
import sys
from anthropic import Anthropic

BUDGET_STATE = pathlib.Path(os.environ.get("BUDGET_STATE_PATH", "/tmp/llm_budget_state.json"))

SYSTEM = """You are the security triage engine for a CI pipeline.

Input: SARIF findings from Semgrep and JSON findings from Trivy.

Output STRICT JSON only, matching this schema:
{
  "findings": [
    {"id": str, "source": "semgrep"|"trivy", "title": str, "severity": "critical"|"high"|"medium"|"low"|"info",
     "verdict": "TRUE_POSITIVE"|"LIKELY_FALSE_POSITIVE"|"NEEDS_HUMAN_REVIEW",
     "why": str, "fix_hint": str, "location": str}
  ],
  "summary": str
}

Rules:
- Only mark TRUE_POSITIVE when the source line, sink, or CVE metadata makes the vulnerability plausible in real execution.
- Mark LIKELY_FALSE_POSITIVE for test files, examples, generated code, or dev-only dependencies.
- Never invent findings.
"""

def load_budget() -> dict:
    if BUDGET_STATE.exists():
        return json.loads(BUDGET_STATE.read_text())
    return {"date": "", "spent_usd": 0.0}

def save_budget(state: dict) -> None:
    BUDGET_STATE.write_text(json.dumps(state))

def enforce_budget(cap: float) -> tuple[str, float]:
    today = dt.date.today().isoformat()
    state = load_budget()
    if state["date"] != today:
        state = {"date": today, "spent_usd": 0.0}
    remaining = cap - state["spent_usd"]
    if remaining <= 0:
        print(f"Budget exhausted for {today}; falling back to haiku", file=sys.stderr)
        return "claude-haiku-4-5", remaining
    if remaining < cap * 0.25:
        return "claude-sonnet-4-5", remaining
    return "claude-opus-4-7", remaining

def record_spend(cost: float) -> None:
    state = load_budget()
    state["spent_usd"] = round(state.get("spent_usd", 0.0) + cost, 6)
    save_budget(state)

def cost(model: str, ti: int, to: int) -> float:
    prices = {
        "claude-opus-4-7": (15.0, 75.0),
        "claude-sonnet-4-5": (3.0, 15.0),
        "claude-haiku-4-5": (0.80, 4.0),
    }
    ip, op = prices[model]
    return (ti / 1_000_000) * ip + (to / 1_000_000) * op

def main() -> int:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--semgrep", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--trivy", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--output", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--markdown", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--daily-budget-usd", type=float, default=5.00)
    args = p.parse_args()

    semgrep = json.loads(pathlib.Path(args.semgrep).read_text()) if pathlib.Path(args.semgrep).exists() else {}
    trivy = json.loads(pathlib.Path(args.trivy).read_text()) if pathlib.Path(args.trivy).exists() else {}

    model, remaining = enforce_budget(args.daily_budget_usd)
    client = Anthropic(api_key=os.environ["ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"])

    user = json.dumps({
        "semgrep_sarif_runs": semgrep.get("runs", [])[:1],
        "trivy_results": trivy.get("Results", [])[:20],
    })[:180_000]

    resp = client.messages.create(
        model=model,
        max_tokens=8000,
        system=SYSTEM,
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": user}],
    )

    spent = cost(model, resp.usage.input_tokens, resp.usage.output_tokens)
    record_spend(spent)

    text = "".join(b.text for b in resp.content if b.type == "text").strip()
    if text.startswith("```"):
        text = text.strip("`").split("\n", 1)[1]
    try:
        parsed = json.loads(text)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        parsed = {"findings": [], "summary": "Triage produced non-JSON output; see raw log."}
        pathlib.Path("triage_raw.txt").write_text(text)

    pathlib.Path(args.output).write_text(json.dumps(parsed, indent=2))

    md = ["# Security triage", "", parsed.get("summary", ""), ""]
    md.append("| id | source | severity | verdict | title |")
    md.append("|----|--------|----------|---------|-------|")
    for f in parsed.get("findings", [])[:50]:
        md.append(f"| {f.get('id','')} | {f.get('source','')} | {f.get('severity','')} | {f.get('verdict','')} | {f.get('title','')} |")
    md.append("")
    md.append(f"*Model: {model}. Spent: ${spent:.4f}. Remaining today: ${max(remaining-spent,0):.4f}.*")
    pathlib.Path(args.markdown).write_text("\n".join(md))
    return 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
4.3 What Fails The Pipeline

Only security_gate fails the build, and only when the LLM has explicitly labeled a finding TRUE_POSITIVE with critical or high severity. Everything else — including LLM errors, budget exhaustion, and NEEDS_HUMAN_REVIEW — produces artifacts but does not block the merge. This bias toward availability is deliberate: a security gate that blocks builds for false positives will be disabled within two sprints.
5. Pattern 3 — Nightly Continuous Assessment on Kali

The CI-triggered patterns above cover code and preview URLs. The nightly cron pattern covers production surface area.
5.1 The Cron Job
# /etc/cron.d/nightly-recon
# Runs at 02:00 local time every day
0 2 * * * pentest /opt/nightly/run.sh >> /var/log/nightly-recon.log 2>&1
5.2 The Runner Script
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# /opt/nightly/run.sh
set -euo pipefail

TS=$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)
BASE=/var/lib/nightly-recon
OUT="$BASE/$TS"
mkdir -p "$OUT"
ln -sfn "$OUT" "$BASE/latest"
PREV=$(ls -1d "$BASE"/2*T*Z 2>/dev/null | sort | tail -n 2 | head -n 1 || true)

SCOPE=/opt/nightly/scope.txt
LOG="$OUT/run.log"
exec >>"$LOG" 2>&1

echo "== Recon start $TS =="

subfinder -dL "$SCOPE" -silent -o "$OUT/subs.txt"
dnsx -l "$OUT/subs.txt" -a -resp-only -silent -o "$OUT/resolved.txt"
httpx -l "$OUT/resolved.txt" -silent -status-code -title -tech-detect -json -o "$OUT/http.jsonl"

nuclei \
  -l <(jq -r '.url' "$OUT/http.jsonl") \
  -severity critical,high,medium \
  -exclude-tags intrusive,dos \
  -rate-limit 40 \
  -jsonl -o "$OUT/nuclei.jsonl" \
  -stats -no-color \
  -timeout 8

if [[ -n "${PREV:-}" && -f "$PREV/nuclei.jsonl" ]]; then
  python3 /opt/nightly/diff_and_triage.py \
    --today "$OUT/nuclei.jsonl" \
    --yesterday "$PREV/nuclei.jsonl" \
    --output "$OUT/diff.md" \
    --slack-webhook "$SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL"
fi

echo "== Recon end $(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ) =="
5.3 The Diff-and-Triage Script
# /opt/nightly/diff_and_triage.py
import argparse
import json
import os
import pathlib
import sys
import urllib.request
from anthropic import Anthropic

SYSTEM = """You are the nightly triage assistant. You compare last night's Nuclei findings with tonight's, and report:
1. Genuinely new findings (not present last night)
2. Findings that disappeared (probably fixed or gone offline)
3. Findings whose severity or evidence changed

Return concise Markdown suitable for Slack. Rank NEW critical/high first. Never invent findings.
"""

def load(path: pathlib.Path) -> list[dict]:
    if not path.exists():
        return []
    out = []
    for line in path.read_text().splitlines():
        line = line.strip()
        if line:
            try:
                out.append(json.loads(line))
            except json.JSONDecodeError:
                pass
    return out

def key(f: dict) -> str:
    return f"{f.get('template-id')}::{f.get('matched-at')}"

def slack_post(webhook: str, text: str) -> None:
    body = json.dumps({"text": text}).encode()
    req = urllib.request.Request(webhook, data=body, headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=10) as r:
        r.read()

def main() -> int:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--today", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--yesterday", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--output", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--slack-webhook", required=True)
    args = p.parse_args()

    today = load(pathlib.Path(args.today))
    yesterday = load(pathlib.Path(args.yesterday))
    t_keys = {key(f): f for f in today}
    y_keys = {key(f): f for f in yesterday}

    new = [t_keys[k] for k in t_keys if k not in y_keys]
    gone = [y_keys[k] for k in y_keys if k not in t_keys]

    if not new and not gone:
        pathlib.Path(args.output).write_text("No delta since last night.\n")
        slack_post(args.slack_webhook, "Nightly recon: no delta since last night.")
        return 0

    client = Anthropic(api_key=os.environ["ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"])
    user = json.dumps({"new": new[:100], "gone": gone[:50]})[:180_000]
    resp = client.messages.create(
        model="claude-sonnet-4-5",
        max_tokens=3000,
        system=SYSTEM,
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": user}],
    )
    md = "".join(b.text for b in resp.content if b.type == "text").strip()
    pathlib.Path(args.output).write_text(md + "\n")
    slack_post(args.slack_webhook, f"*Nightly recon diff*\n{md[:3500]}")
    return 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
5.4 Operational Notes

    Store per-run results under a timestamp directory and keep a latest symlink
    Rotate old runs (keep 90 days) with a nightly find | xargs rm -rf
    Alert if the run itself fails; a silent scanner is worse than no scanner
    If Slack is down, fall back to email; if email is down, write to /var/log and page the on-call

6. Pattern 4 — Retest Workflow Driven by Previous Reports

Every finding you close should be retested. Doing it manually does not scale. The retest pipeline reads the previous engagement's report.md, executes the reproduction steps, and lets the LLM judge whether the finding is still exploitable.
6.1 Report Format Convention

Every finding in the report is a fenced block with a stable id and a reproducible curl command:
### F-023: Reflected XSS in /search
- Severity: high
- Status: fixed
- Reproduce:
```bash
curl -s "https://target.example.com/search?q=<svg/onload=alert(1)>" | grep -o "<svg/onload=alert(1)>"

    Expected (if fixed): no match on grep
    Expected (if vulnerable): grep prints the payload


### 6.2 Retest Driver

```python
# tools/retest.py
import argparse
import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
import sys
from anthropic import Anthropic

BLOCK = re.compile(
    r"### (?P<id>F-\d+): (?P<title>.+?)\n"
    r"- Severity: (?P<sev>\w+)\n"
    r"- Status: (?P<status>\w+)\n"
    r"- Reproduce:\n```bash\n(?P<cmd>.+?)\n```\n"
    r"- Expected \(if fixed\): (?P<fixed>.+?)\n"
    r"- Expected \(if vulnerable\): (?P<vuln>.+?)\n",
    re.DOTALL,
)

SYSTEM = """You are a retest judge. Given the reproduction command, its stdout, its exit code, and the two expected-outcome descriptions, decide:
- STILL_EXPLOITABLE
- FIXED
- INCONCLUSIVE

Return strict JSON: {"verdict": ..., "why": ..., "recommended_action": ...}
"""

def run_repro(cmd: str, timeout: int = 45) -> dict:
    proc = subprocess.run(
        ["bash", "-c", cmd],
        capture_output=True,
        text=True,
        timeout=timeout,
    )
    return {"stdout": proc.stdout[-4000:], "stderr": proc.stderr[-2000:], "exit_code": proc.returncode}

def judge(client: Anthropic, finding: dict, evidence: dict) -> dict:
    user = json.dumps({"finding": finding, "evidence": evidence})
    resp = client.messages.create(
        model="claude-sonnet-4-5",
        max_tokens=600,
        system=SYSTEM,
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": user}],
    )
    text = "".join(b.text for b in resp.content if b.type == "text").strip()
    if text.startswith("```"):
        text = text.strip("`").split("\n", 1)[1]
    try:
        return json.loads(text)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        return {"verdict": "INCONCLUSIVE", "why": "non-json model output", "recommended_action": "manual review"}

def update_jira(ticket: str, verdict: dict) -> None:
    # POST to Jira REST API; omitted here — see /opt/nightly/jira_client.py
    print(f"Jira {ticket}: {verdict}")

def main() -> int:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--report", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--jira-map", required=True, help="JSON: {F-023: JIRA-123}")
    args = p.parse_args()
    report = open(args.report).read()
    ticket_map = json.load(open(args.jira_map))
    client = Anthropic(api_key=os.environ["ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"])

    for m in BLOCK.finditer(report):
        finding = m.groupdict()
        try:
            evidence = run_repro(finding["cmd"])
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            evidence = {"stdout": "", "stderr": "TIMEOUT", "exit_code": -1}
        verdict = judge(client, finding, evidence)
        print(f"{finding['id']} -> {verdict['verdict']}")
        if finding["id"] in ticket_map:
            update_jira(ticket_map[finding["id"]], verdict)
    return 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
6.3 Guardrails

    Retests only run inside the previously agreed scope; the scope file gates every command
    Reproduction commands are executed with a strict timeout
    The judge sees exit code plus truncated stdout; never the raw payload injected into a template
    Every STILL_EXPLOITABLE verdict re-opens a Jira ticket with a link back to the run log

7. Notification Systems

An unreviewed alert is worse than no alert. Route notifications so a human actually reads them.
7.1 Slack Webhook
# tools/notify_slack.py
import json, os, urllib.request

def notify_slack(webhook: str, severity: str, title: str, url: str, body: str) -> None:
    color = {"critical": "#a30000", "high": "#d93f0b", "medium": "#f0b400", "low": "#0e8a16"}.get(severity, "#666")
    payload = {
        "attachments": [{
            "color": color,
            "title": f"[{severity.upper()}] {title}",
            "title_link": url,
            "text": body[:3000],
            "footer": "AI security pipeline",
        }]
    }
    req = urllib.request.Request(webhook, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(), headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=10) as r:
        r.read()
7.2 PagerDuty Events V2
# tools/notify_pagerduty.py
import json, os, urllib.request, uuid

def page(routing_key: str, severity: str, title: str, source: str, details: dict) -> str:
    dedup = str(uuid.uuid4())
    payload = {
        "routing_key": routing_key,
        "event_action": "trigger",
        "dedup_key": dedup,
        "payload": {"summary": title, "severity": severity, "source": source, "custom_details": details},
    }
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        "https://events.pagerduty.com/v2/enqueue",
        data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"},
    )
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=10) as r:
        r.read()
    return dedup
7.3 Discord Webhook
# tools/notify_discord.py
import json, urllib.request

def notify_discord(webhook: str, title: str, body: str) -> None:
    payload = {"embeds": [{"title": title[:255], "description": body[:3900]}]}
    req = urllib.request.Request(webhook, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(), headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=10) as r:
        r.read()
7.4 Per-Severity Routing
# tools/notify.py
from tools.notify_slack import notify_slack
from tools.notify_pagerduty import page
from tools.notify_discord import notify_discord
import os

def route(severity: str, title: str, url: str, body: str) -> None:
    if severity == "critical":
        page(os.environ["PD_ROUTING_KEY"], "critical", title, "ai-pipeline", {"body": body[:1000]})
        notify_slack(os.environ["SLACK_CRIT"], severity, title, url, body)
    elif severity == "high":
        notify_slack(os.environ["SLACK_HIGH"], severity, title, url, body)
    elif severity == "medium":
        notify_slack(os.environ["SLACK_MED"], severity, title, url, body)
    else:
        notify_discord(os.environ["DISCORD_INFO"], title, body)

    Pentester Insight: The single biggest failure mode of security notification is undifferentiated firehose. If every finding lands in the same channel, engineers mute the channel within a week. Split by severity, split by team, and only page for critical.

8. Cost Governance

LLMs are cheap per call and expensive at scale. Governance is what prevents a runaway workflow from producing a five-figure bill.
8.1 Budget Model

    Set a daily cap per project (e.g. $5)
    Set a weekly cap per organization (e.g. $200)
    Set a per-run cap (e.g. $1) as a defence-in-depth control

8.2 Enforcement

The enforce_budget function shown in Section 4.2 is the canonical pattern:

    Read today's spend from a persistent file (in CI, a cache; in production, Redis)
    Below 25 percent remaining, drop from Opus to Sonnet
    Below 0 remaining, drop to Haiku
    Below -10 percent, refuse to call the API and post a warning

8.3 Overspend Alerting
# tools/cost_alert.py
import json, os, pathlib, urllib.request

def check(state_path: str, daily_cap: float, weekly_cap: float, slack_webhook: str) -> None:
    state = json.loads(pathlib.Path(state_path).read_text())
    spent = state.get("spent_usd", 0.0)
    weekly = state.get("weekly_spent_usd", 0.0)
    alerts = []
    if spent > daily_cap:
        alerts.append(f"Daily cap exceeded: ${spent:.2f} > ${daily_cap:.2f}")
    if weekly > weekly_cap * 0.8:
        alerts.append(f"Weekly cap at 80%: ${weekly:.2f} / ${weekly_cap:.2f}")
    if alerts:
        body = {"text": "\n".join(alerts)}
        req = urllib.request.Request(slack_webhook, data=json.dumps(body).encode(), headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=10) as r:
            r.read()
8.4 Fallback Model Selection
Remaining Budget	Model	Cost
100 - 25 percent	claude-opus-4-7	high, best triage
25 - 0 percent	claude-sonnet-4-5	mid, still strong
Below 0	claude-haiku-4-5	low, degraded quality
Below -10 percent	none, refuse	zero

Fallback is not free — the quality drop is real. Design pipelines so a Haiku triage is still useful even if you would prefer Opus.
9. Secret Handling

CI/CD is where API keys leak. Handle secrets with care.
9.1 GitHub Actions

    Store ANTHROPIC_API_KEY in repository secrets, not variables
    Use environment protection rules on production environments so approvals are required
    Never echo a secret; even set -x will leak
    Use OIDC to obtain short-lived cloud credentials rather than long-lived keys

- name: Assume role via OIDC
  uses: aws-actions/configure-aws-credentials@v4
  with:
    role-to-assume: arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/gh-actions-security
    aws-region: us-east-1
9.2 GitLab CI

    Use masked, protected variables
    Prefer file-type variables for JSON credentials
    Store LLM keys in the group scope, not the project scope, when multiple projects share them

9.3 Rotation

    Rotate LLM API keys every 90 days
    Rotate on any suspected exposure (any run with -x on, any push that touched a workflow file, any incident)
    Keep a "break glass" key stored in a hardware token, never in CI

9.4 Detecting Leaked Keys

Add a pre-push hook that greps for common secret prefixes:
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# .git/hooks/pre-push
if git diff --cached | grep -E 'sk-ant-|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}|ghp_[A-Za-z0-9]{36}'; then
    echo "Refusing to push: possible secret in diff" >&2
    exit 1
fi
10. Idempotency and Retries

Every pipeline step must be safe to retry. A run that half-succeeded and left the world in a weird state is worse than a run that failed cleanly.
10.1 Idempotency Rules

    Naming — every artifact includes a run id; never overwrite prior artifacts silently
    Comments — use a marker so a re-run updates the same PR comment instead of creating a duplicate
    Tickets — retest updates existing tickets by ID; never opens a duplicate
    Notifications — dedup key on PagerDuty prevents duplicate pages
    Files — write to a temp path, then mv atomically

10.2 Retry Wrapper
# tools/retry.py
import time, random, functools

def retry(exceptions=(Exception,), tries=3, base=0.5, cap=8.0):
    def deco(fn):
        @functools.wraps(fn)
        def wrapper(*a, **kw):
            for attempt in range(tries):
                try:
                    return fn(*a, **kw)
                except exceptions as e:
                    if attempt == tries - 1:
                        raise
                    sleep = min(cap, base * (2 ** attempt)) + random.uniform(0, 0.25)
                    time.sleep(sleep)
        return wrapper
    return deco

Wrap every network call — Anthropic API, Slack webhook, PagerDuty, Jira. Do not retry on 4xx (except 429), retry on 5xx and transient network errors.
10.3 Poison Pills

If a single input consistently causes the pipeline to crash, quarantine it. Move the input to a poison/ directory, alert once, and continue processing the rest of the batch.
11. Observability

If you can't see it, you can't tune it.
11.1 Structured Logs

Every pipeline step emits one JSON line per action:
# tools/log.py
import json, sys, time, os

def emit(event: str, **fields) -> None:
    row = {"ts": time.time(), "event": event, "run_id": os.environ.get("RUN_ID","-"), **fields}
    sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(row) + "\n")
    sys.stdout.flush()

Example log line:
{"ts": 1757116800.12, "event": "triage.done", "run_id": "gh-123", "input_tokens": 3421, "output_tokens": 812, "cost_usd": 0.0725, "findings_in": 47, "findings_out": 14, "model": "claude-opus-4-7"}
11.2 Trace IDs

Every run gets a UUID. Every downstream log line includes it. Every external HTTP call sends it in a header (X-Run-ID). When you page an on-call at 3am, they can grep one string across every system.
11.3 Prometheus Metrics
# tools/metrics.py
from prometheus_client import Counter, Histogram, push_to_gateway, CollectorRegistry

def push(job: str, gateway: str, cost_usd: float, findings: int, model: str) -> None:
    reg = CollectorRegistry()
    c = Counter("pentest_triage_cost_usd", "Cost per triage run", ["model"], registry=reg)
    c.labels(model=model).inc(cost_usd)
    f = Counter("pentest_findings_total", "Findings observed", ["model"], registry=reg)
    f.labels(model=model).inc(findings)
    push_to_gateway(gateway, job=job, registry=reg)
11.4 Grafana Panels You Actually Want

    Cost per day per project (bar chart)
    Findings per run over time (line chart)
    False positive rate (from human overrides) over time
    P95 pipeline duration
    Notification volume by severity per channel

If a panel does not affect a decision you make, delete it.
12. Full Working Example — security-scan.yml

Bring everything together. The workflow below runs Trivy on a built image, sends the SBOM to Claude for triage, posts a prioritized PR comment, and sets a status check the branch protection rule can require.
# .github/workflows/security-scan.yml
name: Security Scan (Trivy + LLM)

on:
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - "Dockerfile"
      - "requirements.txt"
      - "package-lock.json"
      - "go.sum"
      - "src/**"

permissions:
  contents: read
  pull-requests: write
  checks: write
  id-token: write

concurrency:
  group: security-scan-${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}
  cancel-in-progress: true

jobs:
  build_scan_triage:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 20
    env:
      RUN_ID: ${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}
      ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: ${{ secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY }}
      DAILY_BUDGET_USD: "3.00"
    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Set up Docker Buildx
        uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v3

      - name: Build image
        run: |
          docker build -t local/app:${{ env.RUN_ID }} .

      - name: Install Trivy
        run: |
          curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/aquasecurity/trivy/main/contrib/install.sh | sh -s -- -b /usr/local/bin

      - name: Trivy image scan
        continue-on-error: true
        run: |
          trivy image \
            --format json \
            --output trivy.json \
            --severity HIGH,CRITICAL \
            --ignore-unfixed \
            --scanners vuln,secret,misconfig \
            --timeout 10m \
            local/app:${{ env.RUN_ID }}

      - name: Trivy SBOM (CycloneDX)
        run: |
          trivy image --format cyclonedx --output sbom.json local/app:${{ env.RUN_ID }}

      - name: Set up Python
        uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: "3.12"
          cache: pip

      - name: Install deps
        run: pip install --quiet anthropic==0.34.0

      - name: LLM triage
        id: triage
        run: |
          python tools/triage_trivy.py \
            --trivy trivy.json \
            --sbom sbom.json \
            --output triage.md \
            --json triage.json \
            --daily-budget-usd $DAILY_BUDGET_USD \
            --pr ${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}

      - name: Upload artifacts
        if: always()
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: security-scan-${{ env.RUN_ID }}
          path: |
            trivy.json
            sbom.json
            triage.md
            triage.json
          retention-days: 30

      - name: Sticky PR comment
        uses: actions/github-script@v7
        with:
          github-token: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          script: |
            const fs = require('fs');
            const body = fs.readFileSync('triage.md', 'utf8');
            const marker = '<!-- security-scan-comment -->';
            const { data: comments } = await github.rest.issues.listComments({
              owner: context.repo.owner,
              repo: context.repo.repo,
              issue_number: context.issue.number,
            });
            const existing = comments.find(c => c.body && c.body.includes(marker));
            const finalBody = `${marker}\n\n${body}`;
            if (existing) {
              await github.rest.issues.updateComment({
                owner: context.repo.owner, repo: context.repo.repo,
                comment_id: existing.id, body: finalBody,
              });
            } else {
              await github.rest.issues.createComment({
                owner: context.repo.owner, repo: context.repo.repo,
                issue_number: context.issue.number, body: finalBody,
              });
            }

      - name: Compute status check
        id: check
        run: |
          python - <<'PY'
          import json, os
          data = json.load(open("triage.json"))
          confirmed = [f for f in data.get("findings", []) if f["verdict"] == "TRUE_POSITIVE" and f["severity"] in ("critical","high")]
          if confirmed:
              print(f"::error::{len(confirmed)} confirmed critical/high CVEs")
              conclusion = "failure"
              summary = f"{len(confirmed)} confirmed critical/high CVEs"
          else:
              conclusion = "success"
              summary = "No confirmed critical/high CVEs"
          with open(os.environ["GITHUB_OUTPUT"], "a") as f:
              f.write(f"conclusion={conclusion}\n")
              f.write(f"summary={summary}\n")
          PY

      - name: Post status check
        uses: LouisBrunner/checks-action@v2.0.0
        with:
          token: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          name: security/llm-triage
          conclusion: ${{ steps.check.outputs.conclusion }}
          output: |
            {"summary": "${{ steps.check.outputs.summary }}"}

The companion triage_trivy.py:
# tools/triage_trivy.py
import argparse, json, os, pathlib, sys, datetime as dt
from anthropic import Anthropic

SYSTEM = """You triage container CVEs. For each result, decide:
- TRUE_POSITIVE (real risk given actual usage)
- LIKELY_FALSE_POSITIVE (dev-only, unreachable code path, patched by config)
- NEEDS_HUMAN_REVIEW

Output STRICT JSON:
{"summary": str, "findings": [{"id": str, "cve": str, "package": str, "severity": "critical"|"high"|"medium"|"low",
                                 "verdict": str, "why": str, "fix": str}]}
"""

def main() -> int:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--trivy", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--sbom", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--output", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--json", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--pr", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--daily-budget-usd", type=float, default=3.00)
    args = p.parse_args()

    trivy = json.load(open(args.trivy)) if pathlib.Path(args.trivy).exists() else {}
    sbom = json.load(open(args.sbom)) if pathlib.Path(args.sbom).exists() else {}

    vulns = []
    for res in (trivy.get("Results") or [])[:20]:
        for v in (res.get("Vulnerabilities") or [])[:100]:
            vulns.append({
                "id": v.get("VulnerabilityID"),
                "package": v.get("PkgName"),
                "severity": (v.get("Severity") or "").lower(),
                "fixed": v.get("FixedVersion"),
                "installed": v.get("InstalledVersion"),
                "title": v.get("Title"),
                "cvss": (v.get("CVSS") or {}),
            })

    if not vulns:
        pathlib.Path(args.output).write_text("## Container CVE triage\n\nNo HIGH or CRITICAL CVEs.\n")
        pathlib.Path(args.json).write_text(json.dumps({"summary": "clean", "findings": []}))
        return 0

    client = Anthropic(api_key=os.environ["ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"])
    user = json.dumps({"pr": args.pr, "vulns": vulns[:200]})[:180_000]
    resp = client.messages.create(
        model="claude-sonnet-4-5",
        max_tokens=6000,
        system=SYSTEM,
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": user}],
    )
    text = "".join(b.text for b in resp.content if b.type == "text").strip()
    if text.startswith("```"):
        text = text.strip("`").split("\n", 1)[1]
    try:
        parsed = json.loads(text)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        parsed = {"summary": "non-JSON output", "findings": []}
    pathlib.Path(args.json).write_text(json.dumps(parsed, indent=2))

    md = ["## Container CVE triage", "", parsed.get("summary", ""), ""]
    md += ["| id | package | severity | verdict | fix |", "|----|---------|----------|---------|-----|"]
    for f in parsed.get("findings", [])[:40]:
        md.append(f"| {f.get('id','')} | {f.get('package','')} | {f.get('severity','')} | {f.get('verdict','')} | {f.get('fix','')} |")
    pathlib.Path(args.output).write_text("\n".join(md) + "\n")
    return 0

if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

Add a branch protection rule requiring security/llm-triage to succeed and you have an AI-driven security gate on main.
13. Rollback Safety — How to Disable AI Gates in an Emergency

Sooner or later an LLM misbehaves during a P1 incident. You need a way to turn the AI gate off without a Git push.
13.1 The Kill Switch

Every workflow reads a feature flag at the top:
- name: Kill switch check
  id: killswitch
  run: |
    if [[ "${{ vars.AI_GATE_DISABLED }}" == "true" ]]; then
      echo "AI gate disabled by repository variable AI_GATE_DISABLED"
      echo "disabled=true" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
    fi

- name: LLM triage
  if: steps.killswitch.outputs.disabled != 'true'
  run: python tools/triage_trivy.py ...

Toggling the repository variable AI_GATE_DISABLED to true disables the LLM step within seconds, no code change required.
13.2 Failure Modes and Their Kill Switches
Failure	Kill switch
LLM API down	Automatic fallback to Sonnet -> Haiku -> skip
Runaway cost	Daily budget cap in enforce_budget
False-positive storm	AI_GATE_DISABLED=true variable
Preview URL unreachable	Nuclei step already continue-on-error
Slack webhook down	Fallback route in notify.py
Jira down	Retest verdict logged to file; ticket update deferred
13.3 Post-incident Review

Every kill-switch flip creates a follow-up task automatically. The task must be closed with a written explanation: why the switch was flipped, what fix was made, and when the switch was flipped back. No exceptions.
14. Governance

Security pipelines are, themselves, a security surface. Governance decides who can add gates, who reviews the prompts, and who owns the outages.
14.1 Roles

    Security engineering owns the pipeline templates and the prompts
    Platform engineering owns the CI runners and the secrets store
    Individual product teams may add project-specific templates, subject to review
    On-call owns the kill switch

14.2 Prompt Review

Every prompt used in a pipeline is treated as code:

    Stored in a versioned file under security/prompts/
    Reviewed via pull request
    Has a golden-set test with expected verdicts
    Cannot be changed inside a workflow file inline — the review of a workflow file is not the same as review of a prompt

Example golden-set structure:
security/prompts/
  triage_trivy.md
  triage_nuclei.md
  retest_judge.md
security/goldens/
  triage_trivy/
    01_log4j_true_positive.json
    02_dev_only_false_positive.json
    03_unfixed_no_reachability.json
14.3 Sign-Off For New Gates

A new gate on main requires:

    Written proposal (one page) describing the trigger, the checks, the failure semantics
