2.5 Skill directory layout

Every skill in this lesson lives under ~/.claude/skills/<skill-name>/ while you develop it, and under ~/pentest-tools/skills/<skill-name>/ in the source repo. Symlink from the latter into the former so your edits show up immediately for both Claude Code and Codex CLI:
mkdir -p ~/.claude/skills
mkdir -p ~/pentest-tools/skills/{recon-scanner,vuln-validator,report-generator}
ln -sf ~/pentest-tools/skills/recon-scanner    ~/.claude/skills/recon-scanner
ln -sf ~/pentest-tools/skills/vuln-validator   ~/.claude/skills/vuln-validator
ln -sf ~/pentest-tools/skills/report-generator ~/.claude/skills/report-generator

Confirm Claude Code sees the skills (empty at this point but registered):
claude skills list | grep -E "recon-scanner|vuln-validator|report-generator"
3. Challenge 1 — Recon Scanner Skill
3.1 Requirements

Build recon-scanner/SKILL.md that satisfies every bullet below. A partial implementation loses points even if the prose is beautiful.

    The skill must accept a single input parameter target (a bare domain, e.g. lab.local).
    Before doing anything, it must load engagement/scope.yml (path either from $ENGAGEMENT_SCOPE or from the current working directory), and hard-fail if target is not in in_scope.domains.
    It must chain three ProjectDiscovery tools in order:
        subfinder -silent -d <target> to enumerate subdomains.
        httpx -silent -json over the subdomain list to probe live HTTP(S) services.
        nuclei -silent -tags basic -jsonl over the live URLs.
    Rate must never exceed 5 rps per host (enforce via -rate-limit 5).
    Output must land under ./recon/<target>/ and must include:
        subdomains.txt — raw subfinder output
        httpx.jsonl — raw httpx JSON lines
        nuclei.jsonl — raw nuclei JSON lines
        summary.md — human-readable digest keyed by asset
        summary.json — machine-readable digest for downstream skills
    The skill must be idempotent: running twice against the same target produces the same output tree, and never appends duplicates.
    No secrets or API keys embedded in the SKILL.md.

3.2 Full SKILL.md

Save the following as ~/pentest-tools/skills/recon-scanner/SKILL.md:
---
name: recon-scanner
description: >-
  Scope-aware external reconnaissance. Given a target domain, enumerates
  subdomains with subfinder, probes live HTTP services with httpx, and runs
  a low-noise nuclei sweep tagged "basic". Refuses to run against anything
  not listed in engagement/scope.yml. Writes ./recon/<target>/ with raw
  tool output plus summary.md and summary.json.
version: 1.0.0
author: Red Team Leaders
license: MIT
inputs:
  - name: target
    type: string
    required: true
    description: Bare domain to enumerate. Must appear in engagement scope.
tools_required:
  - subfinder
  - httpx
  - nuclei
  - jq
  - yq
allowed_bash:
  - "subfinder *"
  - "httpx *"
  - "nuclei *"
  - "jq *"
  - "yq *"
  - "mkdir -p *"
  - "sort -u *"
  - "wc -l *"
  - "test *"
---

# recon-scanner

## When to invoke

Use this skill at the beginning of an external engagement, or whenever the
user asks to "recon", "enumerate", "map the attack surface of", or "scan"
a domain that appears in the engagement scope.

Do **not** invoke this skill for internal-only IP ranges — use
`internal-recon` for those. Do not invoke against a target the user has
not explicitly named in this session.

## Preconditions

1. `engagement/scope.yml` exists at `${ENGAGEMENT_SCOPE:-./engagement/scope.yml}`.
2. `target` input parameter is set.
3. `subfinder`, `httpx`, `nuclei`, `jq`, `yq` are on PATH.

## Procedure

Execute the following steps in order. If any step fails, stop and report
the failure to the user with the exact command that failed and its exit
code. Do not attempt to "recover" by skipping steps.

### Step 1 — Load and validate scope

```bash
SCOPE="${ENGAGEMENT_SCOPE:-./engagement/scope.yml}"
test -f "$SCOPE" || { echo "scope file not found: $SCOPE"; exit 2; }

TARGET="{{ target }}"
if ! yq -r '.in_scope.domains[]' "$SCOPE" | grep -qxE "${TARGET}|\*\.${TARGET#*.}"; then
  echo "target ${TARGET} is NOT in engagement scope"; exit 3
fi
Step 2 — Prepare output tree
OUT="./recon/${TARGET}"
mkdir -p "$OUT"
Step 3 — Subdomain enumeration
subfinder -silent -d "$TARGET" -o "$OUT/subdomains.txt.new"
sort -u "$OUT/subdomains.txt.new" > "$OUT/subdomains.txt"
rm -f "$OUT/subdomains.txt.new"
Step 4 — Live host probing
httpx -silent -json -rate-limit 5 \
      -l "$OUT/subdomains.txt" \
      -o "$OUT/httpx.jsonl.new"
sort -u "$OUT/httpx.jsonl.new" > "$OUT/httpx.jsonl"
rm -f "$OUT/httpx.jsonl.new"
Step 5 — Basic vuln sweep
jq -r '.url' "$OUT/httpx.jsonl" > "$OUT/urls.txt"
nuclei -silent -tags basic -jsonl \
       -rate-limit 5 \
       -l "$OUT/urls.txt" \
       -o "$OUT/nuclei.jsonl.new"
sort -u "$OUT/nuclei.jsonl.new" > "$OUT/nuclei.jsonl"
rm -f "$OUT/nuclei.jsonl.new"
Step 6 — Emit summary.json
jq -s --arg target "$TARGET" '{
  target: $target,
  subdomains: [inputs | select(.url) | .input] | unique,
  live_hosts: [inputs | select(.url) | {url: .url, status: .status_code, title: .title, tech: .tech}],
  findings:   [inputs | select(.template_id) | {id: .template_id, severity: .info.severity, url: .matched_at}]
}' "$OUT/httpx.jsonl" "$OUT/nuclei.jsonl" > "$OUT/summary.json"
Step 7 — Emit summary.md

Compose summary.md with the following sections in this exact order:

    # Recon summary — {{ target }}
    ## Subdomains — bulleted list from subdomains.txt.
    ## Live services — table with columns URL | Status | Title | Tech.
    ## Findings — table with columns Template ID | Severity | URL.
    ## Next steps — one-sentence recommendation per severity present.

Postconditions

    ./recon/{{ target }}/summary.md exists and is non-empty.
    ./recon/{{ target }}/summary.json parses as valid JSON.
    Return the path to summary.md to the user.

Failure modes

    Scope violation: exit 3, do not touch the filesystem beyond mkdir.
    Tool missing: exit 4, name the tool.
    Zero subdomains: emit an empty but well-formed summary.md with a "no subdomains discovered" note; do not skip subsequent steps.


### 3.3 Verification

Bring up the lab stack, populate a local DNS override so `lab.local` resolves onto the Docker bridge, and invoke the skill:

```bash
echo "172.26.0.10 lab.local juice.lab.local"  | sudo tee -a /etc/hosts
echo "172.26.0.11 dvwa.lab.local"             | sudo tee -a /etc/hosts
echo "172.26.0.12 vulnnode.lab.local"         | sudo tee -a /etc/hosts

cd ~/pentest-tools
ENGAGEMENT_SCOPE=./engagement/scope.yml \
  claude skills run recon-scanner --input target=lab.local

Confirm the output tree:
tree recon/lab.local/
# recon/lab.local/
# ├── httpx.jsonl
# ├── nuclei.jsonl
# ├── subdomains.txt
# ├── summary.json
# ├── summary.md
# └── urls.txt

jq -e '.target == "lab.local"' recon/lab.local/summary.json
test -s recon/lab.local/summary.md

Negative test — the skill must refuse an out-of-scope target:
claude skills run recon-scanner --input target=example.com
# Expected: exit 3, message "target example.com is NOT in engagement scope"

Idempotency test — a second invocation must not double the output:
BEFORE=$(wc -l < recon/lab.local/subdomains.txt)
claude skills run recon-scanner --input target=lab.local
AFTER=$(wc -l < recon/lab.local/subdomains.txt)
test "$BEFORE" -eq "$AFTER"
4. Challenge 2 — Vuln Validator Skill
4.1 Requirements

Build vuln-validator/SKILL.md that satisfies every bullet below.

    The skill accepts one input finding — either a path to a single nuclei JSON line file, or a raw JSON string.
    Before executing anything, it must confirm the target URL is reachable from the current network (single-shot HEAD).
    It must reproduce the exact request the nuclei template issued, using curl -v so both the request and the response are dumpable.
    It must save the evidence bundle under ./findings/<slug>/ where <slug> = <template_id>-<host>-<sha1[:8]>.
    The bundle must contain:
        finding.yml — canonical metadata (template ID, severity, host, URL, matched_at, matcher name, tags, timestamp).
        request.http — the raw HTTP request that reproduced the finding.
        response.http — the raw response, headers plus body (truncated to 64 KiB).
        screenshot.png — if chromium is available on PATH, a headless screenshot of the URL.
    The skill must degrade gracefully: missing chromium skips the screenshot but does not fail the run.
    If reachability check fails, the skill exits with a machine-readable status and does not create the bundle.

4.2 Full SKILL.md

Save the following as ~/pentest-tools/skills/vuln-validator/SKILL.md:
---
name: vuln-validator
description: >-
  Takes a raw nuclei finding and produces a reproducible evidence bundle.
  Verifies the target is reachable, replays the offending request with
  curl, captures the response, and (when chromium is available) grabs a
  screenshot. Emits ./findings/<slug>/ with finding.yml, request.http,
  response.http, screenshot.png. Safe to invoke unattended.
version: 1.0.0
author: Red Team Leaders
license: MIT
inputs:
  - name: finding
    type: string
    required: true
    description: >-
      Path to a nuclei JSONL file (single line) or a raw JSON string
      containing one nuclei finding.
tools_required:
  - curl
  - jq
  - yq
  - sha1sum
optional_tools:
  - chromium
allowed_bash:
  - "curl *"
  - "jq *"
  - "yq *"
  - "sha1sum *"
  - "chromium *"
  - "mkdir -p *"
  - "head -c *"
  - "test *"
  - "printf *"
---

# vuln-validator

## When to invoke

Use this skill after `recon-scanner` (or any other nuclei run) has
produced JSONL findings you want to promote from "raw signal" to
"reportable evidence". Also use it whenever the user says "validate",
"reproduce", "confirm", or "prove" for a specific finding.

Do **not** use this skill to run arbitrary requests — the request comes
from the nuclei finding, not from the user.

## Preconditions

1. `finding` input is set (path or inline JSON).
2. `curl`, `jq`, `yq`, `sha1sum` on PATH.
3. Working directory is writable.

## Procedure

### Step 1 — Parse the finding

```bash
IN="{{ finding }}"
if test -f "$IN"; then
  RAW=$(cat "$IN")
else
  RAW="$IN"
fi

TEMPLATE_ID=$(echo "$RAW" | jq -r '.template_id')
SEVERITY=$(echo "$RAW"    | jq -r '.info.severity')
MATCHED_AT=$(echo "$RAW"  | jq -r '.matched_at')
HOST=$(echo "$MATCHED_AT" | awk -F/ '{print $3}')
METHOD=$(echo "$RAW"      | jq -r '.request // "GET" | tostring | split("\n")[0] | split(" ")[0] // "GET"')
REQBODY=$(echo "$RAW"     | jq -r '.request // ""')
SLUG_HASH=$(echo -n "$RAW" | sha1sum | cut -c1-8)
SLUG="${TEMPLATE_ID}-${HOST}-${SLUG_HASH}"
OUT="./findings/${SLUG}"
mkdir -p "$OUT"
Step 2 — Reachability check
if ! curl -s -o /dev/null -m 5 -w "%{http_code}\n" "$MATCHED_AT" | grep -qE "^[23][0-9]{2}$|^401$|^403$"; then
  printf "unreachable: %s\n" "$MATCHED_AT" > "$OUT/.unreachable"
  echo "UNREACHABLE"; exit 5
fi
Step 3 — Reproduce the request
curl -sk -o "$OUT/response.body" \
     -D "$OUT/response.headers" \
     --trace-ascii "$OUT/curl.trace" \
     -m 15 -X "$METHOD" "$MATCHED_AT"

# Assemble request.http from the trace (or from the nuclei-provided request)
if test -n "$REQBODY"; then
  printf "%s\n" "$REQBODY" > "$OUT/request.http"
else
  awk '/^=> Send header/{flag=1;next} /^<=|^== Info/{flag=0} flag {print}' \
      "$OUT/curl.trace" > "$OUT/request.http"
fi

# Assemble response.http = headers + blank line + first 64 KiB of body
{ cat "$OUT/response.headers"; echo; head -c 65536 "$OUT/response.body"; } > "$OUT/response.http"
rm -f "$OUT/curl.trace" "$OUT/response.body" "$OUT/response.headers"
Step 4 — Screenshot (best effort)
if command -v chromium >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  chromium --headless --disable-gpu --no-sandbox \
           --window-size=1280,900 \
           --screenshot="$OUT/screenshot.png" \
           "$MATCHED_AT" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
fi
Step 5 — Emit finding.yml
cat > "$OUT/finding.yml" <<YAML
template_id: "${TEMPLATE_ID}"
severity:    "${SEVERITY}"
host:        "${HOST}"
url:         "${MATCHED_AT}"
matched_at:  "${MATCHED_AT}"
slug:        "${SLUG}"
captured_at: "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)"
evidence:
  request:    "request.http"
  response:   "response.http"
  screenshot: "$([ -f "$OUT/screenshot.png" ] && echo screenshot.png || echo null)"
YAML
Postconditions

    ./findings/<slug>/finding.yml parses as valid YAML.
    ./findings/<slug>/request.http and response.http exist and are non-empty.
    If chromium was available, ./findings/<slug>/screenshot.png exists and is a valid PNG.

Failure modes

    Malformed finding JSON: exit 2, name the missing field.
    Unreachable target: exit 5, write .unreachable sentinel, do not create request/response files.
    curl timeout: exit 6, do not create finding.yml.


### 4.3 Verification

Pick one line out of the nuclei output from Challenge 1 and feed it in:

```bash
cd ~/pentest-tools
head -1 recon/lab.local/nuclei.jsonl > /tmp/one-finding.jsonl

claude skills run vuln-validator --input finding=/tmp/one-finding.jsonl

# Confirm bundle
SLUG=$(ls findings/ | head -1)
test -f "findings/$SLUG/finding.yml"
test -s "findings/$SLUG/request.http"
test -s "findings/$SLUG/response.http"
yq -r '.template_id' "findings/$SLUG/finding.yml"

Negative test — a finding pointing at an unreachable host must exit 5 and leave no bundle other than the sentinel:
echo '{"template_id":"tech-detect","info":{"severity":"info"},"matched_at":"http://172.26.0.99/"}' \
  > /tmp/dead.jsonl
claude skills run vuln-validator --input finding=/tmp/dead.jsonl
# Expected exit 5, .unreachable file present, no request.http/response.http.

Screenshot-degradation test — temporarily rename chromium and re-run; the skill must succeed without producing screenshot.png:
sudo mv /usr/bin/chromium /usr/bin/chromium.bak
claude skills run vuln-validator --input finding=/tmp/one-finding.jsonl
sudo mv /usr/bin/chromium.bak /usr/bin/chromium
5. Challenge 3 — Report Generator Skill
5.1 Requirements

Build report-generator/SKILL.md that satisfies every bullet below.

    The skill accepts optional inputs findings_dir (default ./findings) and out (default ./report).
    It walks every finding.yml under findings_dir, one directory deep.
    For each finding it computes a CVSS 4.0 base score using the vector stored in the finding, or defaults to a severity-to-score table when the vector is absent.
    It drafts a two-paragraph executive summary that names the client (from engagement/scope.yml), the count of findings by severity, and the highest-risk theme.
    It assembles a Markdown report report.md with sections: Cover, Executive Summary, Scope, Methodology, Findings (one subsection per finding), Appendix.
    Each finding subsection embeds the request/response snippets (first 40 lines each) and, if a screenshot exists, references it via a relative image link.
    If typst is on PATH and a report.typ template exists alongside the skill, the skill invokes typst compile and produces report.pdf.
    The skill must be safe to re-run: it overwrites report.md and report.pdf but never touches the source findings/.

5.2 Full SKILL.md

Save the following as ~/pentest-tools/skills/report-generator/SKILL.md:
---
name: report-generator
description: >-
  Reads every findings/*/finding.yml, computes CVSS 4.0 base scores, drafts
  an executive summary, assembles a Markdown pentest report, and (if typst
  is available) renders a PDF. Reads engagement/scope.yml for client
  metadata. Never modifies the source findings tree.
version: 1.0.0
author: Red Team Leaders
license: MIT
inputs:
  - name: findings_dir
    type: string
    required: false
    default: "./findings"
    description: Directory to walk for finding.yml files.
  - name: out
    type: string
    required: false
    default: "./report"
    description: Directory to write report.md and report.pdf into.
tools_required:
  - yq
  - jq
  - python3
optional_tools:
  - typst
allowed_bash:
  - "yq *"
  - "jq *"
  - "python3 *"
  - "typst *"
  - "mkdir -p *"
  - "ls *"
  - "find *"
  - "test *"
  - "wc *"
  - "head *"
  - "cp *"
---

# report-generator

## When to invoke

Use this skill at the end of an engagement, once `vuln-validator` has
produced one or more `findings/<slug>/finding.yml` bundles. Also use it
whenever the user says "draft the report", "compile the report",
"executive summary", or "render the pentest report".

Do **not** use this skill to invent findings — every subsection must
trace back to a `finding.yml` on disk.

## Preconditions

1. `findings_dir` exists and contains at least one `finding.yml`.
2. `engagement/scope.yml` exists (for client name, window, code).
3. `yq`, `jq`, `python3` on PATH.

## Procedure

### Step 1 — Prepare output tree

```bash
FDIR="{{ findings_dir | default('./findings') }}"
OUT="{{ out | default('./report') }}"
mkdir -p "$OUT/evidence"
Step 2 — Load scope
SCOPE="${ENGAGEMENT_SCOPE:-./engagement/scope.yml}"
CLIENT=$(yq -r '.engagement.client'       "$SCOPE")
CODE=$(yq -r   '.engagement.code'         "$SCOPE")
WINDOW_START=$(yq -r '.engagement.window_start' "$SCOPE")
WINDOW_END=$(yq -r   '.engagement.window_end'   "$SCOPE")
Step 3 — Enumerate findings and score them
python3 - <<'PY' "$FDIR" "$OUT/findings.json"
import sys, json, glob, os
try:
    import yaml
except ImportError:
    o​s.system("pip install --quiet pyyaml")
    import yaml

SEV_FALLBACK = {"critical": 9.5, "high": 8.0, "medium": 5.5, "low": 3.0, "info": 0.0}

def cvss4_from_vector(v):
    # Minimal CVSS 4.0 scoring: this is a lab implementation; the real
    # calculator lives in a separate helper skill. We approximate by
    # mapping the base metrics to the standard rubric.
    if not v or "CVSS:4.0" not in v:
        return None
    parts = dict(x.split(":") for x in v.split("/")[1:] if ":" in x)
    weights = {"AV":{"N":0.85,"A":0.62,"L":0.55,"P":0.2},
               "AC":{"L":0.77,"H":0.44},
               "PR":{"N":0.85,"L":0.62,"H":0.27},
               "UI":{"N":0.85,"P":0.62,"A":0.27}}
    impact = {"H":0.56,"L":0.22,"N":0.0}
    exploit = 8.22 * weights["AV"].get(parts.get("AV","N"),0.85) \
                   * weights["AC"].get(parts.get("AC","L"),0.77) \
                   * weights["PR"].get(parts.get("PR","N"),0.85) \
                   * weights["UI"].get(parts.get("UI","N"),0.85)
    isc = 1 - ((1-impact.get(parts.get("VC","N"),0.0))
              *(1-impact.get(parts.get("VI","N"),0.0))
              *(1-impact.get(parts.get("VA","N"),0.0)))
    isc = 7.52*(isc - 0.029) - 3.25*((isc - 0.02)**15)
    if isc <= 0: return 0.0
    return round(min(10.0, exploit + isc), 1)

out = []
for f in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(sys.argv[1], "*", "finding.yml"))):
    with open(f) as fh:
        y = yaml.safe_load(fh)
    vec = y.get("cvss_vector")
    score = cvss4_from_vector(vec) if vec else SEV_FALLBACK.get(y.get("severity","info").lower(), 0.0)
    y["cvss4_score"] = score
    y["source_dir"]  = os.path.dirname(f)
    out.append(y)

with open(sys.argv[2], "w") as fh:
    json.dump(out, fh, indent=2)
print(f"loaded {len(out)} findings", file=sys.stderr)
PY
Step 4 — Draft executive summary
python3 - <<'PY' "$OUT/findings.json" "$OUT/summary.md" "$CLIENT" "$CODE"
import sys, json, collections
findings = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))
client, code = sys.argv[3], sys.argv[4]
bysev = collections.Counter(f["severity"].lower() for f in findings)
top   = sorted(findings, key=lambda f: -f.get("cvss4_score", 0.0))[:3]
top_names = ", ".join(f["template_id"] for f in top) or "no reportable findings"

with open(sys.argv[2], "w") as fh:
    fh.write(f"## Executive summary\n\n")
    fh.write(f"Red Team Leaders performed an external penetration test for "
             f"{client} under engagement {code}. Across the test window we "
             f"identified {len(findings)} findings: "
             f"{bysev.get('critical',0)} critical, {bysev.get('high',0)} high, "
             f"{bysev.get('medium',0)} medium, {bysev.get('low',0)} low, and "
             f"{bysev.get('info',0)} informational.\n\n")
    fh.write(f"The highest-risk themes were: {top_names}. These are the "
             f"issues we recommend prioritising first; each is reproducible "
             f"from the evidence bundled with this report.\n")
PY
Step 5 — Assemble report.md
python3 - <<'PY' "$OUT/findings.json" "$OUT/summary.md" "$OUT/report.md" "$SCOPE"
import sys, json, os, yaml, textwrap
findings = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))
summary  = open(sys.argv[2]).read()
scope    = yaml.safe_load(open(sys.argv[3]))
client   = scope["engagement"]["client"]
code     = scope["engagement"]["code"]

def snippet(path, n=40):
    if not os.path.exists(path): return "(missing)"
    with open(path, errors="replace") as fh:
        return "".join(fh.readlines()[:n])

with open(sys.argv[2].replace("summary.md","report.md"), "w") as fh:
    fh.write(f"# Penetration Test Report — {client}\n\n")
    fh.write(f"Engagement code: **{code}**\n\n")
    fh.write(f"Prepared by Red Team Leaders.\n\n---\n\n")
    fh.write(summary + "\n\n---\n\n")
    fh.write("## Scope\n\n")
    for d in scope["in_scope"]["domains"]:
        fh.write(f"- {d}\n")
    fh.write("\n## Methodology\n\n")
    fh.write("Reconnaissance was performed with the `recon-scanner` skill "
             "(subfinder + httpx + nuclei -tags basic). Each candidate finding "
             "was promoted to a validated finding using the `vuln-validator` "
             "skill, which reproduces the request with curl and captures the "
             "response and (when available) a headless-chromium screenshot.\n\n")
    fh.write("## Findings\n\n")
    for f in sorted(findings, key=lambda x: -x.get("cvss4_score",0.0)):
        fh.write(f"### {f['template_id']} — {f['severity'].upper()} (CVSS4 {f['cvss4_score']})\n\n")
        fh.write(f"- Host: `{f['host']}`\n")
        fh.write(f"- URL: `{f['url']}`\n")
        fh.write(f"- Captured: {f['captured_at']}\n\n")
        fh.write("#### Request\n\n```http\n")
        fh.write(snippet(os.path.join(f["source_dir"], "request.http")))
        fh.write("```\n\n#### Response\n\n```http\n")
        fh.write(snippet(os.path.join(f["source_dir"], "response.http")))
        fh.write("```\n\n")
        shot = os.path.join(f["source_dir"], "screenshot.png")
        if os.path.exists(shot):
            fh.write(f"![screenshot]({shot})\n\n")
    fh.write("## Appendix\n\nAll raw tool output is preserved under `./recon/` and `./findings/`.\n")
PY
Step 6 — Optional: render PDF via typst
if command -v typst >/dev/null 2>&1 && test -f "$(dirname "$0")/report.typ"; then
  cp "$(dirname "$0")/report.typ" "$OUT/report.typ"
  typst compile "$OUT/report.typ" "$OUT/report.pdf" || echo "typst compile failed (non-fatal)"
fi
Postconditions

    ${out}/report.md exists and starts with # Penetration Test Report.
    ${out}/findings.json exists and is a JSON array.
    If typst succeeded, ${out}/report.pdf exists.

Failure modes

    findings_dir empty: emit report.md with an "empty engagement" note; do not fail.
    Malformed finding.yml: skip that finding, log its path to stderr, keep going.
    typst missing: skip PDF, do not fail.


### 5.3 Verification

Fix two lab findings in place before running the skill so the test is deterministic:

```bash
mkdir -p ~/pentest-tools/findings/tech-detect-172.26.0.10-aaaaaaaa
cat > ~/pentest-tools/findings/tech-detect-172.26.0.10-aaaaaaaa/finding.yml <<YAML
template_id: tech-detect
severity: info
host: 172.26.0.10
url: http://172.26.0.10/
matched_at: http://172.26.0.10/
slug: tech-detect-172.26.0.10-aaaaaaaa
captured_at: "2026-09-05T09:00:00Z"
cvss_vector: "CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/VC:N/VI:N/VA:N"
YAML
printf 'GET / HTTP/1.1\nHost: 172.26.0.10\n\n' > ~/pentest-tools/findings/tech-detect-172.26.0.10-aaaaaaaa/request.http
printf 'HTTP/1.1 200 OK\nServer: nginx\n\n<html>Juice Shop</html>\n' > ~/pentest-tools/findings/tech-detect-172.26.0.10-aaaaaaaa/response.http

mkdir -p ~/pentest-tools/findings/xss-dvwa-172.26.0.11-bbbbbbbb
cat > ~/pentest-tools/findings/xss-dvwa-172.26.0.11-bbbbbbbb/finding.yml <<YAML
template_id: dvwa-reflected-xss
severity: high
host: 172.26.0.11
url: http://172.26.0.11/vulnerabilities/xss_r/?name=%3Cscript%3Ealert(1)%3C%2Fscript%3E
matched_at: http://172.26.0.11/vulnerabilities/xss_r/
slug: xss-dvwa-172.26.0.11-bbbbbbbb
captured_at: "2026-09-05T09:05:00Z"
cvss_vector: "CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:P/VC:L/VI:L/VA:N"
YAML
printf 'GET /vulnerabilities/xss_r/?name=<script>alert(1)</script> HTTP/1.1\nHost: 172.26.0.11\n\n' > ~/pentest-tools/findings/xss-dvwa-172.26.0.11-bbbbbbbb/request.http
printf 'HTTP/1.1 200 OK\nContent-Type: text/html\n\n<pre>Hello <script>alert(1)</script></pre>\n' > ~/pentest-tools/findings/xss-dvwa-172.26.0.11-bbbbbbbb/response.http

Run the skill and assert the output structure:
cd ~/pentest-tools
claude skills run report-generator --input findings_dir=./findings --input out=./report

test -f report/report.md
grep -q "^# Penetration Test Report" report/report.md
grep -q "Executive summary" report/report.md
grep -q "dvwa-reflected-xss" report/report.md
grep -q "tech-detect"        report/report.md
jq -e 'length == 2' report/findings.json

If a report.typ exists next to the skill, confirm the PDF renders:
test -f report/report.pdf && echo "PDF rendered" || echo "PDF skipped (expected if no report.typ)"
6. Testing Methodology

Skills fail in three characteristic ways: the frontmatter is wrong and the loader refuses them, the body is right but does the wrong thing on odd inputs, or the body is right for one target but non-idempotent across two. Test at three levels.
6.1 Schema tests (loader-level)

Every skill you write must parse cleanly by the loader. Add a tiny harness at ~/pentest-tools/skills/tests/schema_test.sh:
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
for s in recon-scanner vuln-validator report-generator; do
  claude skills validate "$s" >/dev/null
  echo "OK: $s"
done

Run bash tests/schema_test.sh — expect three OK lines.
6.2 Unit tests (fixture-driven)

Every skill has one or two decision points that are easy to isolate and cheap to test. Feed fixture inputs, assert on side-effects.

For recon-scanner, the decision point is scope validation. Write a fixture scope file, invoke the skill against an in-scope and an out-of-scope target, and check exit codes:
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# tests/recon_scope_test.sh
set -euo pipefail
export ENGAGEMENT_SCOPE=./tests/fixtures/scope.yml
mkdir -p tests/fixtures
cat > tests/fixtures/scope.yml <<'YAML'
engagement: {code: TEST, client: T, window_start: "", window_end: ""}
in_scope:  {domains: [example.test], cidrs: [], urls: []}
out_of_scope: {domains: [], cidrs: []}
rules: []
YAML
if claude skills run recon-scanner --input target=example.test >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "in-scope PASS"
else
  echo "in-scope FAIL"; exit 1
fi
if claude skills run recon-scanner --input target=evil.test >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "out-of-scope FAIL"; exit 1
else
  echo "out-of-scope PASS"
fi

For vuln-validator, the decision point is reachability. Point it at a reachable and an unreachable URL and assert on the .unreachable sentinel.

For report-generator, the decision point is finding enumeration. Point it at an empty findings_dir and assert the report still assembles with an "empty engagement" note.
6.3 Integration tests (live lab)

Once schema and unit tests pass, run the skills against the Docker lab end-to-end. Wire the three skills together in a single script — this is exactly the pattern Module 07 uses:
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# tests/integration_full_stack.sh
set -euo pipefail
cd ~/pentest-tools
claude skills run recon-scanner --input target=lab.local
for line in $(cat recon/lab.local/nuclei.jsonl); do
  echo "$line" > /tmp/f.jsonl
  claude skills run vuln-validator --input finding=/tmp/f.jsonl || true
done
claude skills run report-generator --input findings_dir=./findings --input out=./report
test -f report/report.md
echo "integration OK"

Anything you would put behind CI at a real client engagement lives in this script.
6.4 Regression testing

Every time you change a SKILL.md, re-run all three test tiers. Skills drift silently — a frontmatter field renamed by the loader in a new Claude Code release will not error, it will simply be ignored, and your skill will start doing something subtly wrong. Pin the CLI versions in tool-versions.txt and pin the skill schemas by keeping the tests green on every commit.
7. Skill Packaging (Distribution as a Plugin)

Claude Code accepts a plugin as a directory containing a plugin.json and one or more skills/. Once you have the three skills passing tests, package them so a teammate can install the whole set in one command.
7.1 Directory layout
~/pentest-tools/plugins/rtl-pentest/
├── plugin.json
├── README.md
└── skills/
    ├── recon-scanner/
    │   └── SKILL.md
    ├── vuln-validator/
    │   └── SKILL.md
    └── report-generator/
        ├── SKILL.md
        └── report.typ
7.2 plugin.json
{
  "name": "rtl-pentest",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "description": "Red Team Leaders pentest skill pack: recon-scanner, vuln-validator, report-generator.",
  "author": "Red Team Leaders",
  "license": "MIT",
  "homepage": "https://github.com/red-team-leaders/rtl-pentest",
  "requires": {
    "claude-code": ">=1.0.0",
    "codex-cli":  ">=0.9.0"
  },
  "external_tools": [
    "subfinder", "httpx", "nuclei", "curl", "jq", "yq", "python3", "chromium", "typst"
  ],
  "skills": [
    "skills/recon-scanner",
    "skills/vuln-validator",
    "skills/report-generator"
  ]
}
7.3 Local install
claude plugins install ~/pentest-tools/plugins/rtl-pentest
claude plugins list | grep rtl-pentest
claude skills list | grep -E "recon-scanner|vuln-validator|report-generator"
7.4 Publish to a git remote
cd ~/pentest-tools
git add plugins/rtl-pentest
git commit -m "feat(plugin): package rtl-pentest v1.0.0"
git remote add origin git@github.com:<you>/pentest-tools.git
git push -u origin main

A teammate installs it with:
claude plugins install git+https://github.com/<you>/pentest-tools#subdirectory=plugins/rtl-pentest
7.5 Versioning discipline

Bump plugin.json version on every SKILL.md change. Tag the git commit with the same version:
git tag -a rtl-pentest-v1.0.0 -m "rtl-pentest 1.0.0"
git push origin rtl-pentest-v1.0.0

Semver applies at the skill level too: a change to the input schema is a major bump; a new optional input is a minor; a fix to the SKILL.md body is a patch.
8. Independent Challenges

These are open-ended. Each should take between two and six hours. Do not open a PR until the tests pass locally.
8.1 Passive OSINT skill

Build passive-osint/SKILL.md that never touches the target. Chain any three of: crt.sh certificate transparency, dnsx passive resolution, chaos API, Wayback Machine URL enumeration, GitHub dorks via the API. Emit ./osint/<target>/summary.md with the same shape as recon-scanner. The rubric is: nothing you do must produce a packet the target's WAF could log.
8.2 Report-diff skill

Build report-diff/SKILL.md that consumes two report.md files (an initial engagement and a retest) and produces diff.md showing which findings are fixed, still open, changed severity, and newly introduced. The rubric is: the diff must be reproducible from the two inputs alone — no hidden state.
8.3 MCP-wrapped skill

Wrap the nuclei-templates-server MCP server as a skill entry point named templates-lookup. The skill accepts a cve input, calls the MCP server to fetch the matching template, and prints the template body. The rubric is: the SKILL.md must declare the MCP server in its mcp_servers frontmatter and must fail cleanly when the MCP server is not connected (recall from Module 06 Lesson 09 that connection failure is a run-time condition, not a configuration error).
9. Grading Rubric
Item	Max	Pass Criteria
Environment set up correctly	5	docker compose ps shows three healthy services; claude skills list shows the three skills
recon-scanner SKILL.md valid	10	claude skills validate recon-scanner exits 0
recon-scanner scope enforcement	10	Out-of-scope target exits 3 without writing files
recon-scanner output structure	10	summary.md and summary.json present, well-formed, idempotent
vuln-validator SKILL.md valid	10	claude skills validate vuln-validator exits 0
vuln-validator evidence bundle	10	finding.yml, request.http, response.http all present and non-empty
vuln-validator graceful degradation	5	Missing chromium does not fail the run
report-generator SKILL.md valid	10	claude skills validate report-generator exits 0
report-generator report structure	10	report.md has cover, exec summary, scope, methodology, findings, appendix
report-generator CVSS enrichment	5	Findings sorted by CVSS 4.0 score in the report
Testing methodology applied	5	tests/*.sh all green
Plugin packaging	5	claude plugins install .../rtl-pentest succeeds; skills discoverable
Independent challenges (any 1 of 3)	5	Solution present, tested, documented
Total	100	Pass mark: 75
10. Submitting Your Work

The submission target is your personal ~/pentest-tools/skills/ git repository. Every subsequent module assumes this repo exists and is up to date.
cd ~/pentest-tools
git add skills/ plugins/ tests/ tool-versions.txt engagement/scope.yml
git status

Expected untracked-or-modified list:
skills/recon-scanner/SKILL.md
skills/vuln-validator/SKILL.md
skills/report-generator/SKILL.md
skills/report-generator/report.typ         (optional)
plugins/rtl-pentest/plugin.json
plugins/rtl-pentest/README.md
tests/schema_test.sh
tests/recon_scope_test.sh
tests/integration_full_stack.sh
tool-versions.txt
engagement/scope.yml

Commit with a semantic message:
git commit -m "feat(module-06): recon-scanner, vuln-validator, report-generator v1.0.0"

Push to your remote:
git push origin main
git tag -a module-06-complete -m "Module 06 lab complete"
git push origin module-06-complete

Verify one more time that a fresh checkout on a clean host can run all three tests:
cd /tmp
git clone git@github.com:<you>/pentest-tools.git
cd pentest-tools
bash tests/schema_test.sh
bash tests/recon_scope_test.sh
bash tests/integration_full_stack.sh

If those three commands all exit 0, you have finished the lab.

Submit the following artefacts to your course portal:

    The commit SHA of the module-06-complete tag.
    A screenshot of claude skills list showing the three skills registered.
    The generated report/report.md from the integration test.
    A short paragraph describing which of the three independent challenges you attempted and how you tested it.

11. Summary and Pointer to Module 07

You now have three skills in ~/.claude/skills/ that between them cover the full external-engagement loop: enumerate, validate, report. You have a test harness that catches loader-level breakage, a fixture-driven suite that catches decision-point regressions, and an integration script that exercises the whole chain against a Dockerised lab. You have packaged the three skills as a distributable Claude Code plugin, tagged a v1.0.0, and pushed it to a git remote your teammates can install from.

The skills are deliberately simple. They wrap well-known primitives (subfinder, httpx, nuclei, curl, typst), and they lean on Claude Code's SKILL.md contract for orchestration. That simplicity is the point: the value is not in the tool wrappers, it is in the discipline of scope enforcement, idempotent output layout, reproducible evidence bundles, and a report that traces every claim back to a file on disk.

Module 07 (Real Client Engagements) picks up here. Every walkthrough in Module 07 starts by invoking recon-scanner against the client's in-scope domain, promotes selected findings via vuln-validator, and closes with report-generator. If you have not built and tested all three of these skills, the Module 07 walkthroughs will fail at the first step.

Before you leave this lesson, run through the checklist one last time:

    docker compose ps on ~/labs/module06 shows three healthy services.
    claude skills list shows recon-scanner, vuln-validator, and report-generator.
    bash tests/schema_test.sh prints three OK lines.
    bash tests/integration_full_stack.sh exits 0 and produces report/report.md.
    git log --oneline shows a feat(module-06) commit tagged module-06-complete.
    Your ~/pentest-tools/skills/ git remote is up to date.

Everything green? Move on to Module 07 — Real Client Engagements.
